{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"call_function_rc0t65gm9ov3_3","type":"tool_result","content":"1\t\"\"\"Warp backend conventions for Phase 1 GPU acceleration.\n2\t\n3\tThis module establishes the foundational backend contracts that all kernel modules\n4\tmust follow. It provides:\n5\t\n6\t- Device management and GPU selection policies\n7\t- Persistent array base types and allocation conventions\n8\t- Module stability markers for JIT compilation\n9\t- Capture-safe stepping protocol\n10\t- Profiling hooks and instrumentation\n11\t\n12\tDesign Principles\n13\t----------------\n14\t1. **Module stability before JIT churn**: All kernels in a module compile together.\n15\t   Stabilize module contents early to avoid reload churn.\n16\t2. **Persistent arrays over temporaries**: Field buffers are allocated once and\n17\t   reused across timesteps to minimize allocation overhead.\n18\t3. **Capture-safe stepping**: Kernels must not capture mutable state from the\n19\t   outer Python scope. All timestep-dependent values must be passed as arguments.\n20\t4. **Explicit device placement**: Arrays carry device affinity explicitly rather\n21\t   than relying on global state.\n22\t5. **Structured profiling**: Use Warp's ScopedTimer and NVTX markers for\n23\t   GPU-side instrumentation, with CPU-side timing hooks for end-to-end metrics.\n24\t\n25\tDevice Management\n26\t-----------------\n27\t- Use ``get_warp_device()`` to obtain the default device or a specific GPU.\n28\t- For multi-GPU: map logical chunk indices to device IDs via\n29\t  ``ChunkLayout.device_assignment``.\n30\t- All array allocations use the target device explicitly.\n31\t\n32\tArray Conventions\n33\t----------------\n34\t- Field arrays: ``wp.array(dtype=wp.float32, shape=(Nx, Ny, Nz, 3))``\n35\t- Coefficient arrays: ``wp.array(dtype=wp.float32, shape=(Nx, Ny, Nz))``\n36\t- Auxiliary state: ``wp.array(dtype=wp.float32, shape=(num_poles, Nx, Ny, Nz))``\n37\t- All arrays are allocated on the target device; no managed memory unless explicit.\n38\t\n39\tDtype Policy\n40\t-----------\n41\t- Single precision (float32/complex64) is the default for GPU execution.\n42\t- Double precision (float64/complex128) is available but may significantly\n43\t  reduce performance on consumer NVIDIA GPUs.\n44\t- Complex fields are required when any Bloch boundary is present.\n45\t- The ``requires_complex_fields`` flag from ``BoundarySpecIR`` determines whether\n46\t  to use complex dtype for field storage.\n47\t\n48\tModule Stability\n49\t---------------\n50\tUse ``mark_module_stable()`` after defining all kernels in a module.\n51\tThis signals that the module's kernel set is frozen and ready for JIT.\n52\tDuring development, unstable modules trigger a warning on import.\n53\t\n54\tCapture-Safe Stepping\n55\t---------------------\n56\tEvery kernel function that depends on timestep-varying values (dt, time, step_index)\n57\tmust receive those values as explicit arguments. Do NOT capture them from closure.\n58\t\n59\tExample::\n60\t\n61\t    # BAD - captures dt from outer scope\n62\t    @wp.kernel\n63\t    def bad_update(E, H,):\n64\t        dt = outer_dt  # captured!\n65\t        ...\n66\t\n67\t    # GOOD - dt passed explicitly\n68\t    @wp.kernel\n69\t    def good_update(E, H, dt: float):\n70\t        ...\n71\t\n72\tCapture-safe stepping also means:\n73\t- Source amplitudes are computed outside the kernel and passed as arrays or scalars\n74\t- Monitor intervals are evaluated in Python before calling recording kernels\n75\t- Boundary coefficients are pre-compiled and passed as array arguments\n76\t\n77\tProfiling Hooks\n78\t---------------\n79\t- ``WarpTimer`` context manager for GPU-side kernel timing\n80\t- ``nvtx_range`` for annotating execution phases\n81\t- ``step_metrics()`` helper for computing cells/s and Gcells/s\n82\t\n83\tReferences\n84\t----------\n85\t- Warp docs: https://docs.nvidia.com/warp-py\n86\t- GPU Benchmarking: ``../papers/gpu_benchmarking.pdf``\n87\t- Chunk Contract: ``../phase1/architecture/chunk-contract.md``\n88\t\"\"\"\n89\t\n90\tfrom __future__ import annotations\n91\t\n92\tfrom contextlib import contextmanager\n93\tfrom dataclasses import dataclass, field\n94\tfrom typing import Any, Literal\n95\t\n96\t__all__ = [\n97\t    \"WARP_AVAILABLE\",\n98\t    \"wp\",\n99\t    \"get_warp_device\",\n100\t    \"get_default_device\",\n101\t    \"allocate_field_array\",\n102\t    \"allocate_coefficient_array\",\n103\t    \"allocate_auxiliary_array\",\n104\t    \"FieldArrayTag\",\n105\t    \"CoefficientArrayTag\",\n106\t    \"AuxiliaryArrayTag\",\n107\t    \"ArrayTags\",\n108\t    \"WarpTimer\",\n109\t    \"nvtx_range\",\n110\t    \"step_metrics\",\n111\t    \"backend_info\",\n112\t    \"WarpBackendInfo\",\n113\t    \"ComplexFieldPolicy\",\n114\t    \"ModuleStability\",\n115\t    \"mark_module_stable\",\n116\t    \"is_module_stable\",\n117\t]\n118\t\n119\t\n120\t# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n121\t# Warp import with graceful fallback\n122\t# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n123\t\n124\ttry:\n125\t    import warp as wp\n126\texcept ModuleNotFoundError:  # backend conventions for Phase 1 GPU acceleration.\n\nThis module establishes the foundational backend contracts that all kernel modules\nmust follow. It provides:\n\n- Device management and GPU selection policies\n- Persistent array base types and allocation conventions\n- Module stability markers for JIT compilation\n- Capture-safe stepping protocol\n- Profiling hooks and instrumentation\n\nDesign Principles\n----------------\n1. **Module stability before JIT churn**: All kernels in a module compile together.\n   Stabilize module contents early to avoid reload churn.\n2. **Persistent arrays over temporaries**: Field buffers are allocated once and\n   reused across timesteps to minimize allocation overhead.\n3. **Capture-safe stepping**: Kernels must not capture mutable state from the\n   outer Python scope. All timestep-dependent values must be passed as arguments.\n4. **Explicit device placement**: Arrays carry device affinity explicitly rather\n   than relying on global state.\n5. **Structured profiling**: Use Warp's ScopedTimer and NVTX markers for\n   GPU-side instrumentation, with CPU-side timing hooks for end-to-end metrics.\n\nDevice Management\n-----------------\n- Use ``get_warp_device()`` to obtain the default device or a specific GPU.\n- For multi-GPU: map logical chunk indices to device IDs via\n  ``ChunkLayout.device_assignment``.\n- All array allocations use the target device explicitly.\n\nArray Conventions\n----------------\n- Field arrays: ``wp.array(dtype=wp.float32, shape=(Nx, Ny, Nz, 3))``\n- Coefficient arrays: ``wp.array(dtype=wp.float32, shape=(Nx, Ny, Nz))``\n- Auxiliary state: ``wp.array(dtype=wp.float32, shape=(num_poles, Nx, Ny, Nz))``\n- All arrays are allocated on the target device; no managed memory unless explicit.\n\nDtype Policy\n-----------\n- Single precision (float32/complex64) is the default for GPU execution.\n- Double precision (float64/complex128) is available but may significantly\n  reduce performance on consumer NVIDIA GPUs.\n- Complex fields are required when any Bloch boundary is present.\n- The ``requires_complex_fields`` flag from ``BoundarySpecIR`` determines whether\n  to use complex dtype for field storage.\n\nModule Stability\n---------------\nUse ``mark_module_stable()`` after defining all kernels in a module.\nThis signals that the module's kernel set is frozen and ready for JIT.\nDuring development, unstable modules trigger a warning on import.\n\nCapture-Safe Stepping\n---------------------\nEvery kernel function that depends on timestep-varying values (dt, time, step_index)\nmust receive those values as explicit arguments. Do NOT capture them from closure.\n\nExample::\n\n    # BAD - captures dt from outer scope\n    @wp.kernel\n    def bad_update(E, H,):\n        dt = outer_dt  # captured!\n        ...\n\n    # GOOD - dt passed explicitly\n    @wp.kernel\n    def good_update(E, H, dt: float):\n        ...\n\nCapture-safe stepping also means:\n- Source amplitudes are computed outside the kernel and passed as arrays or scalars\n- Monitor intervals are evaluated in Python before calling recording kernels\n- Boundary coefficients are pre-compiled and passed as array arguments\n\nProfiling Hooks\n---------------\n- ``WarpTimer`` context manager for GPU-side kernel timing\n- ``nvtx_range`` for annotating execution phases\n- ``step_metrics()`` helper for computing cells/s and Gcells/s\n\nReferences\n----------\n- Warp docs: https://docs.nvidia.com/warp-py\n- GPU Benchmarking: ``../papers/gpu_benchmarking.pdf``\n- Chunk Contract: ``../phase1/architecture/chunk-contract.md``\n\"\"\"\n\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\nfrom contextlib import contextmanager\nfrom dataclasses import dataclass, field\nfrom typing import Any, Literal\n\n__all__ = [\n    \"WARP_AVAILABLE\",\n    \"wp\",\n    \"get_warp_device\",\n    \"get_default_device\",\n    \"allocate_field_array\",\n    \"allocate_coefficient_array\",\n    \"allocate_auxiliary_array\",\n    \"FieldArrayTag\",\n    \"CoefficientArrayTag\",\n    \"AuxiliaryArrayTag\",\n    \"ArrayTags\",\n    \"WarpTimer\",\n    \"nvtx_range\",\n    \"step_metrics\",\n    \"backend_info\",\n    \"WarpBackendInfo\",\n    \"ComplexFieldPolicy\",\n    \"ModuleStability\",\n    \"mark_module_stable\",\n    \"is_module_stable\",\n]\n\n\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n# Warp import with graceful fallback\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\ntry:\n    import warp as wp\nexcept ModuleNotFoundError:  # pragma: no cover - \"\"\"Compute sum of |E|^2 over the entire field array.\n\n    Works with both numpy arrays and Warp arrays (GPU).\n    For Warp arrays, copies data to CPU for the computation.\n    \"\"\"\n    if Import locally to avoid circular imports\n    from autofdtd.compiler.monitors import (\n        FieldMonitorState,\n        FluxMonitorState,\n        MediumMonitorState,\n        ModeMonitorState,\n    )\n    from autofdtd.runtime.monitors import (\n        record_monitor_fields,\n        record_monitor_flux,\n    )\n    from autofdtd.runtime.sources import apply_source_injection_stage\n\n    import time as time_module\n\n    # Determine step budget\n    if max_steps is None:\n        max_steps = compiled.runtime_controls.num_time_steps\n\n    dt = compiled.runtime_controls.dt\n    cell_sizes = (\n        compiled.resolved_grid.x.cell_sizes[0]\n        if compiled.resolved_grid.x.cell_sizes\n        else 1.0,\n        compiled.resolved_grid.y.cell_sizes[0]\n        if compiled.resolved_grid.y.cell_sizes\n        else 1.0,\n        compiled.resolved_grid.z.cell_sizes[0]\n        if compiled.resolved_grid.z.cell_sizes\n        else 1.0,\n    )\n    dx, dy, dz = cell_sizes\n\n    # Allocate field state\n    field_state = allocate_field_state(compiled)\n\n    # Initialize source runtime states\n    source_runtimes = _initialize_source_runtimes(compiled)\n\n    # Initialize monitor states\n    monitor_states = _initialize_monitor_states(compiled)\n\n    # Timing\n    wall_start = time_module.perf_counter()\n\n    # Main timestep loop\n    for step in range(max_steps):\n        # 1. Electric field update\n        step_result = step_maxwell(\n            {\n                \"E\": field_state.E,\n                \"H\": field_state.H,\n                \"eps_xx\": field_state.eps_xx,\n                \"eps_yy\": field_state.eps_yy,\n                \"eps_zz\": field_state.eps_zz,\n                \"mu_xx\": field_state.mu_xx,\n                \"mu_yy\": field_state.mu_yy,\n                \"mu_zz\": field_state.mu_zz,\n            },\n            dt=dt,\n            dx=dx,\n            dy=dy,\n            dz=dz,\n            step_index=step,\n            time=current_time,\n        )\n\n        # 3. Compute integrated electric field for convergence check\n        E_sq = _compute_field_magnitude_sq(field_state.E)\n        integrated_electric = E_sq\n        integrated_electric_history.append(integrated_electric)\n\n        # Update peak for convergence\n        if integrated_electric > peak_integrated_electric:\n            peak_integrated_electric = integrated_electric\n\n        # 4. Check convergence / stop conditions\n        shutoff_ratio = 1.0\n        if peak_integrated_electric > 0.0:\n            shutoff_ratio = integrated_electric / peak_integrated_electric\n\n        stop_reason = \"max_steps\"\n        if shutoff_ratio <= float(compiled.runtime_controls.shutoff) and step > 0:\n            stop_reason = \"shutoff\"\n            num_steps_executed = step + 1\n            current_time = (step + 1) * dt\n            break\n\n        if step == max_steps - 1:\n            stop_reason = \"max_steps\"\n            num_steps_executed = step + 1\n            current_time = (step + 1) * dt\n            break\n\n        # 5. Record monitor data at intervals\n        if step % record_interval == 0:\n            _record_monitor_data(\n                monitor_states,\n                field_state,\n                compiled,\n                current_time,\n                step,\n            )\n\n        # 6. Progress reporting\n        if verbose and step % progress_interval == 0:\n            print(\n                f\"Step {step}/{max_steps} | \"\n                f\"Time {current_time:.3e} | \"\n                f\"|E|² {integrated_electric:.3e} | \"\n                f\"Shutoff {shutoff_ratio:.3e}\"\n            )\n\n        current_time += dt\n\n    # Final timing\n    wall_end = time_module.perf_counter()\n    wall_time = wall_end - wall_start\n\n    # Final monitor recording\n    _record_monitor_data(\n        monitor_states,\n        field_state,\n        compiled,\n        current_time,\n        max_steps - 1,\n    )\n\n    # Extract monitor data\n    field_monitor_data = _extract_field_monitor_data(\n        monitor_states, compiled.resolved_grid\n    )\n    flux_monitor_data = _extract_flux_monitor_data(\n        monitor_states, compiled.resolved_grid\n    )\n    medium_monitor_data = _extract_medium_monitor_data(\n        monitor_states, compiled.resolved_grid\n    )\n    mode_monitor_data = _extract_mode_monitor_data(\n        monitor_states, compiled.resolved_grid\n    )\n\n    # Compute metrics\n    total_cells = compiled.total_cells\n    gcells_per_second = (\n        (total_cells * num_steps_executed / wall_time) / 1e9\n        if wall_time > 0\n        else 0.0\n    )\n\n    return ExecutionResult(\n        field_state=field_state,\n        num_steps=num_steps_executed,\n        final_time=current_time,\n        stop_reason=stop_reason,\n        integrated_electric_history=integrated_electric_history,\n        field_monitor_data=field_monitor_data,\n        flux_monitor_data=flux_monitor_data,\n        medium_monitor_data=medium_monitor_data,\n        mode_monitor_data=mode_monitor_data,\n        metrics={\n            \"wall_time_s\": wall_time,\n            \"cells_updated\": total_cells * num_steps_executed,\n            \"gcells_per_second\": gcells_per_second,\n            \"backend\": \"numpy\" if not WARP_AVAILABLE else \"warp\",\n        },\n    )\n\n\ndef _initialize_source_runtimes(compiled) -> dict[str, Any]:\n    \"\"\"Initialize runtime source data structures.\"\"\"\n    runtimes = {}\n\n    # \"coefficient_requires\": (\"eps_xx\", \"eps_yy\", \"eps_zz\", \"mu_xx\", \"mu_yy\", \"mu_zz\"),\n182\t    }\n183\t\n184\t\n185\t# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n186\t# Warp kernel definitions\n187\t# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n188\t\n189\t# These are the core Maxwell update kernels. They are defined using Warp's\n190\t# @wp.kernel decorator for JIT compilation. The actual implementations follow\n191\t# the Yee lattice update equations documented in this module's docstring.\n192\t\n193\tif WARP_AVAILABLE:\n194\t\n195\t    # Component indices for field arrays with shape (nx, ny, nz, 3)\n196\t    _EX, _EY, _EZ = 0, 1, 2\n197\t    _HX, _HY, _HZ = 0, 1, 2\n198\t\n199\t    @wp.kernel\n200\t    def electric_update_3d(\n201\t        E: wp.array(dtype=wp.float32, ndim=4),\n202\t        H: wp.array(dtype=wp.float32, ndim=4),\n203\t        eps_xx: wp.array(dtype=wp.float32, ndim=3),\n204\t        eps_yy: wp.array(dtype=wp.float32, ndim=3),\n205\t        eps_zz: wp.array(dtype=wp.float32, ndim=3),\n206\t        dt: float,\n207\t        dx: float,\n208\t        dy: float,\n209\t        dz: float,\n210\t        nx: int,\n211\t        ny: int,\n212\t        nz: int,\n213\t    ):\n214\t        \"\"\"Update electric field components for 3D Yee lattice.\n215\t\n216\t        This kernel updates Ex, Ey, Ez at interior cells using the\n217\t        curl of the magnetic field. Ghost cells must be filled by\n218\t        boundary exchange before calling this kernel.\n219\t\n220\t        Array layout: E and H have shape (nx, ny, nz, 3) with\n221\t        component order (Ex=0, Ey=1, Ez=2) for E and (Hx=0, Hy=1, Hz=2) for H.\n222\t\n223\t        Yee lattice curl formulas (Faraday's law, vacuum):\n224\t            Ex: dHz/dy - dHy/dz\n225\t            Ey: dHx/dz - dHz/dx\n226\t            Ez: dHy/dx - dHx/dy\n227\t\n228\t        Each component update uses neighbor H values at offset positions\n229\t        corresponding to the Yee cell staggering.\n230\t\n231\t        Args:\n232\t            E: Electric field array with shape (nx, ny, nz, 3), component order (Ex, Ey, Ez)\n233\t            H: Magnetic field array with shape (nx, ny, nz, 3), component order (Hx, Hy, Hz)\n234\t            eps_xx, eps_yy, eps_zz: Permittivity arrays with shape (nx, ny, nz)\n235\t            dt: Timestep size\n236\t            dx, dy, dz: Cell sizes\n237\t            nx, ny, nz: Grid dimensions\n238\t        \"\"\"\n239\t        i, j, k = wp.tid()\n240\t\n241\t        # Bounds check - update interior cells only (skip ghost cells at boundaries)\n242\t        # Valid interior indices: i in [1, nx-2], j in [1, ny-2], k in [1, nz-2]\n243\t        if i < 1 or i >= nx - 1 or j < 1 or j >= ny - 1 or k < 1 or k >= nz - 1:\n244\t            return\n245\t\n246\t        # ---- Ex component update ----\n247\t        # Ex at (i, j, k) uses Hz at (i, j, k) and (i, j+1, k) for dHz/dy\n248\t        # and Hy at (i, j, k) and (i, j, k+1) for dHy/dz\n249\t        # dHz_dy = (Hz[i, j+1, k, Hz] - Hz[i, j, k, Hz]) / dy\n250\t        # dHy_dz = (Hy[i, j, k+1, Hy] - Hy[i, j, k, Hy]) / dz\n251\t        Hz_at_ij = H[i, j, k, _HZ]\n252\t        Hz_at_ip1j = H[i, j + 1, k, _HZ]\n253\t        Hy_at_ijk = H[i, j, k, _HY]\n254\t        Hy_at_ijkp1 = H[i, j, k + 1, _HY]\n255\t\n256\t        dHz_dy_Ex = (Hz_at_ip1j - Hz_at_ij) / dy\n257\t        dHy_dz_Ex = (Hy_at_ijkp1 - Hy_at_ijk) / dz\n258\t\n259\t        eps_Ex = eps_xx[i, j, k]\n260\t        ce_Ex = dt / (eps_Ex + 1e-20)\n261\t        E[i, j, k, _EX] += ce_Ex * (dHz_dy_Ex - dHy_dz_Ex)\n262\t\n263\t        # ---- Ey component update ----\n264\t        # Ey at (i, j, k) uses Hz at (i, j, k) and (i+1, j, k) for dHz/dx\n265\t        # and Hx at (i, j, k) and (i, j, k+1) for dHx/dz\n266\t        Hz_at_ijk_Ey = H[i, j, k, _HZ]\n267\t        Hz_at_ip1jk = H[i + 1, j, k, _HZ]\n268\t        Hx_at_ijk_Ey = H[i, j, k, _HX]\n269\t        Hx_at_ijkp1 = H[i, j, k + 1, _HX]\n270\t\n271\t        dHz_dx_Ey = (Hz_at_ip1jk - Hz_at_ijk_Ey) / dx\n272\t        dHx_dz_Ey = (Hx_at_ijkp1 - Hx_at_ijk_Ey) / dz\n273\t\n274\t        eps_Ey = eps_yy[i, j, k]\n275\t        ce_Ey = dt / (eps_Ey + 1e-20)\n276\t        E[i, j, k, _EY] += ce_Ey * (dHz_dx_Ey - dHx_dz_Ey)\n277\t\n278\t        # ---- Ez component update ----\n279\t        # Ez at (i, j, k) uses Hy at (i, j, k) and (i+1, j, k) for dHy/dx\n280\t        # and Hx at (i, j, k) and (i, j+1, k) for dHx/dy\n281\t        Hy_at_ijk_Ez = H[i, j, k, _HY]\n282\t        Hy_at_ip1jk = H[i + 1, j, k, _HY]\n283\t        Hx_at_ijk_Ez = H[i, j, k, _HX]\n284\t        Hx_at_ijp1k = H[i, j + 1, k, _HX]\n285\t\n286\t        dHy_dx_Ez = (Hy_at_ip1jk - Hy_at_ijk_Ez) / dx\n287\t        dHx_dy_Ez = (Hx_at_ijp1k - Hx_at_ijk_Ez) / dy\n288\t\n289\t        eps_Ez = eps_zz[i, j, k]\n290\t        ce_Ez = dt / (eps_Ez + 1e-20)\n291\t        E[i, j, k, _EZ] += ce_Ez * (dHy_dx_Ez - dHx_dy_Ez)\n292\t\n293\t    @wp.kernel\n294\t    def magnetic_update_3d(\n295\t        H: wp.array(dtype=wp.float32, ndim=4),\n296\t        E: wp.array(dtype=wp.float32, ndim=4),\n297\t        mu_xx: wp.array(dtype=wp.float32, ndim=3),\n298\t        mu_yy: wp.array(dtype=wp.float32, ndim=3),\n299\t        mu_zz: wp.array(dtype=wp.float32, ndim=3),\n300\t        dt: float,\n301\t        dx: float,\n302\t        dy: float,\n303\t        dz: float,\n304\t        nx: int,\n305\t        ny: int,\n306\t        nz: int,\n307\t    ):\n308\t        \"\"\"Update magnetic field components for 3D Yee lattice.\n309\t\n310\t        This kernel updates Hx, Hy, Hz at interior cells using the\n311\t        curl of the electric field. Ghost cells must be filled by\n312\t        boundary exchange before calling this kernel.\n313\t\n314\t        Array layout: E and H have shape (nx, ny, nz, 3) with\n315\t        component order (Ex=0, Ey=1, Ez=2) for E and (Hx=0, Hy=1, Hz=2) for H.\n316\t\n317\t        Yee lattice curl formulas (Ampere-Maxwell law, vacuum):\n318\t            Hx: dEy/dz - dEz/dy\n319\t            Hy: dEz/dx - dEx/dz\n320\t            Hz: dEx/dy - dEy/dx\n321\t\n322\t        Each component update uses neighbor E values at offset positions\n323\t        corresponding to the Yee cell staggering.\n324\t\n325\t        Args:\n326\t            H: Magnetic field array with shape (nx, ny, nz, 3), component order (Hx, Hy, Hz)\n327\t            E: Electric field array with shape (nx, ny, nz, 3), component order (Ex, Ey, Ez)\n328\t            mu_xx, mu_yy, mu_zz: Permeability arrays with shape (nx, ny, nz)\n329\t            dt: Timestep size\n330\t            dx, dy, dz: Cell sizes\n331\t            nx, ny, nz: Grid dimensions\n332\t        \"\"\"\n333\t        i, j, k = wp.tid()\n334\t\n335\t        # Bounds check - update interior cells only (skip ghost cells at boundaries)\n336\t        if i < 1 or i >= nx - 1 or j < 1 or j >= ny - 1 or k < 1 or k >= nz - 1:\n337\t            return\n338\t\n339\t        # ---- Hx component update ----\n340\t        # Hx at (i, j, k) uses Ey at (i, j, k) and (i, j, k+1) for dEy/dz\n341\t        # and Ez at (i, j, k) and (i, j+1, k) for dEz/dy\n342\t        # Hx_update = -dt/mu * (dEy_dz - dEz_dy)\n343\t        Ey_at_ijk_Hx = E[i, j, k, _EY]\n344\t        Ey_at_ijkp1 = E[i, j, k + 1, _EY]\n345\t        Ez_at_ijk_Hx = E[i, j, k, _EZ]\n346\t        Ez_at_ijp1k = E[i, j + 1, k, _EZ]\n347\t\n348\t        dEy_dz_Hx = (Ey_at_ijkp1 - Ey_at_ijk_Hx) / dz\n349\t        dEz_dy_Hx = (Ez_at_ijp1k - Ez_at_ijk_Hx) / dy\n350\t\n351\t        mu_Hx = mu_xx[i, j, k]\n352\t        ch_Hx = -dt / (mu_Hx + 1e-20)\n353\t        H[i, j, k, _HX] += ch_Hx * (dEy_dz_Hx - dEz_dy_Hx)\n354\t\n355\t        # ---- Hy component update ----\n356\t        # Hy at (i, j, k) uses Ez at (i, j, k) and (i+1, j, k) for dEz/dx\n357\t        # and Ex at (i, j, k) and (i, j, k+1) for dEx/dz\n358\t        Ez_at_ijk_Hy = E[i, j, k, _EZ]\n359\t        Ez_at_ip1jk = E[i + 1, j, k, _EZ]\n360\t        Ex_at_ijk_Hy = E[i, j, k, _EX]\n361\t        Ex_at_ijkp1 = E[i, j, k + 1, _EX]\n362\t\n363\t        dEz_dx_Hy = (Ez_at_ip1jk - Ez_at_ijk_Hy) / dx\n364\t        dEx_dz_Hy = (Ex_at_ijkp1 - Ex_at_ijk_Hy) / dz\n365\t\n366\t        mu_Hy = mu_yy[i, j, k]\n367\t        ch_Hy = -dt / (mu_Hy + 1e-20)\n368\t        H[i, j, k, _HY] += ch_Hy * (dEz_dx_Hy - dEx_dz_Hy)\n369\t\n370\t        # ---- Hz component update ----\n371\t        # Hz at (i, j, k) uses Ex at (i, j, k) and (i, j+1, k) for dEx/dy\n372\t        # and Ey at (i, j, k) and (i+1, j, k) for dEy/dx\n373\t        Ex_at_ijk_Hz = E[i, j, k, _EX]\n374\t        Ex_at_ijp1k = E[i, j + 1, k, _EX]\n375\t        Ey_at_ijk_Hz = E[i, j, k, _EY]\n376\t        Ey_at_ip1jk = E[i + 1, j, k, _EY]\n377\t\n378\t        dEx_dy_Hz = (Ex_at_ijp1k - Ex_at_ijk_Hz) / dy\n379\t        dEy_dx_Hz = (Ey_at_ip1jk - Ey_at_ijk_Hz) / dx\n380\t\n381\t        mu_Hz = mu_zz[i, j, k]\n382\t        ch_Hz = -dt / (mu_Hz + 1e-20)\n383\t        H[i, j, k, _HZ] += ch_Hz * (dEx_dy_Hz - dEy_dx_Hz)\n384\t\n385\t    # ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n386\t    # Coefficient-aware update kernels (inhomogeneous materials)\n387\t    # ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n388\t\n389\t    @wp.kernel\n390\t    def electric_update_3d_coeff(\n391\t        E: wp.array(dtype=wp.float32, ndim=4),\n392\t        H: wp.array(dtype=wp.float32, ndim=4),\n393\t        e_decay_xx: wp.array(dtype=wp.float32, ndim=3),\n394\t        e_decay_yy: wp.array(dtype=wp.float32, ndim=3),\n395\t        e_decay_zz: wp.array(dtype=wp.float32, ndim=3),\n396\t        e_drive_xx: wp.array(dtype=wp.float32, ndim=3),\n397\t        e_drive_yy: wp.array(dtype=wp.float32, ndim=3),\n398\t        e_drive_zz: wp.array(dtype=wp.float32, ndim=3),\n399\t        electric_modes: wp.array(dtype=wp.float32, ndim=3),\n400\t        dt: float,\n401\t        dx: float,\n402\t        dy: float,\n403\t        dz: This kernel updates Ex, Ey, Ez at interior cells using precomputed\n411\t        decay and drive coefficient arrays that already account for spatially\n412\t        varying permittivity, conductivity, and PEC/PMC clamp modes.\n413\t\n414\t        The coefficient arrays are assembled by ``assemble_coefficient_fields``\n415\t        in the discretization pipeline, so this kernel does not sample media\n416\t        directly — it uses the compiled coefficients directly.\n417\t\n418\t        Array layout: E and H have shape (nx, ny, nz, 3) with\n419\t        component order (Ex=0, Ey=1, Ez=2) electric_modes: Array of constitutive mode codes (0=standard, 1=clamp_zero).\n427\t            dt: Timestep size.\n428\t            dx, dy, dz: Cell sizes.\n429\t            nx, ny, nz: Grid dimensions.\n430\t        \"\"\"\n431\t        i, j, k = wp.tid()\n432\t\n433\t        # Bounds check - update interior cells only\n434\t        if i < 1 or i >= nx - 1 or j < 1 or j >= ny - 1 or k < Hy_at_ijk = H[i, j, k, _HY]\n441\t        Hy_at_ijkp1 = H[i, j, k + 1, _HY]\n442\t\n443\t        dHz_dy_Ex = (Hz_at_ip1j - Hz_at_ij) / dy\n444\t        dHy_dz_Ex = (Hy_at_ijkp1 - Hy_at_ijk) / dz\n445\t\n446\t        mode_Ex = int(electric_modes[i, j, k])\n447\t        if mode_Ex == 1:  # CLAMP_ZERO\n448\t            E[i, j, k, _EX] = 0.0\n449\t        else:\n450\t            curl_Ex = dHz_dy_Ex - dHy_dz_Ex\n451\t            E[i, j, k, _EX] = e_decay_xx[i, j, k] * E[i, j, k, _EX] + e_drive_xx[i, j, k] * curl_Ex\n452\t\n453\t        # ---- Ey component update ----\n454\t        Hz_at_ijk_Ey = H[i, j, k, _HZ]\n455\t        Hz_at_ip1jk = H[i + 1, j, k, _HZ]\n456\t        Hx_at_ijk_Ey = H[i, j, k, _HX]\n457\t        Hx_at_ijkp1 = H[i, j, k + 1, _HX]\n458\t\n459\t        dHz_dx_Ey = (Hz_at_ip1jk - Hz_at_ijk_Ey) / dx\n460\t        dHx_dz_Ey = (Hx_at_ijkp1 - Hx_at_ijk_Ey) / dz\n461\t\n462\t        mode_Ey = int(electric_modes[i, j, k])\n463\t        if mode_Ey == 1:  # Hx_at_ijk_Ez = H[i, j, k, _HX]\n473\t        Hx_at_ijp1k = H[i, j + 1, k, _HX]\n474\t\n475\t        dHy_dx_Ez = (Hy_at_ip1jk - Hy_at_ijk_Ez) / dx\n476\t        dHx_dy_Ez = (Hx_at_ijp1k - Hx_at_ijk_Ez) / dy\n477\t\n478\t        mode_Ez = int(electric_modes[i, j, k])\n479\t        if mode_Ez == 1:  # CLAMP_ZERO\n480\t            E[i, j, k, _EZ] = 0.0\n481\t        else:\n482\t            curl_Ez = dHy_dx_Ez - dHx_dy_Ez\n483\t            E[i, j, k, _EZ] = e_decay_zz[i, j, k] * E[i, j, k, _EZ] + e_drive_zz[i, j, k] * curl_Ez\n484\t\n485\t    @wp.kernel\n486\t    def dEx_dy_Hz = (Ex_at_ijp1k - Ex_at_ijk_Hz) / dy\n572\t        dEy_dx_Hz = (Ey_at_ip1jk - Ey_at_ijk_Hz) / dx\n573\t\n574\t        mode_Hz = int(magnetic_modes[i, j, k])\n575\t        if mode_Hz == 1:  # CLAMP_ZERO\n576\t            H[i, j, k, _HZ] = 0.0\n577\t        else:\n578\t            curl_Hz = dEx_dy_Hz - dEy_dx_Hz\n579\t            H[i, j, k, _HZ] = m_decay_zz[i, j, k] * H[i, j, k, _HZ] + m_drive_zz[i, j, k] * curl_Hz\n580\t\n581\t    # ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n582\t    # Dispersive (PoleResidue) update kernels\n583\t    # ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n584\t\n585\t    @wp.kernel\n586\t    def pole_residue_electric_update_3d(\n587\t        E: wp.array(dtype=wp.float32, e_decay_zz: wp.array(dtype=wp.float32, ndim=3),\n592\t        e_drive_xx: wp.array(dtype=wp.float32, ndim=3),\n593\t        e_drive_yy: wp.array(dtype=wp.float32, ndim=3),\n594\t        e_drive_zz: wp.array(dtype=wp.float32, ndim=3),\n595\t        electric_modes: wp.array(dtype=wp.float32, ndim=3),\n596\t        P: wp.array(dtype=wp.float32, ndim=4),\n597\t        pole_decays: float,\n603\t        dz: float,\n604\t        nx: int,\n605\t        ny: int,\n606\t        nz: int,\n607\t    ):\n608\t        \"\"\"Update electric fields with PoleResidue dispersive polarization.\n609\t\n610\t        Uses a staged update: baseline constitutive update plus per-pole\n611\t        polarization correction via auxiliary state array P with shape\n612\t        ``(num_poles, nx, ny, nz)``.\n613\t\n614\t        Args:\n615\t            E: Electric field array (nx, ny, nz, 3).\n616\t            H: Magnetic field array (nx, ny, nz, 3).\n617\t            e_decay_xx/yy/zz: Baseline E decay coefficients.\n618\t            e_drive_xx/yy/zz: Baseline E drive coefficients.\n619\t            electric_modes: Constitutive mode array.\n620\t            P: Polarization state array (num_poles, nx, ny, nz).\n621\t            pole_decays: Per-pole complex decay factors (num_poles,).\n622\t            pole_drives: Per-pole complex drive factors (num_poles,).\n623\t            num_poles: Number of poles.\n624\t            dt: Timestep size.\n625\t            dx, dy, dz: Cell sizes.\n626\t            nx, ny, nz: Grid dimensions.\n627\t        \"\"\"\n628\t        i, j, k = wp.tid()\n629\t\n630\t        if i < 1 or i >= nx - 1 or j < 1 or j >= ny - 1 or k < 1 or k >= nz - 1:\n631\t            return\n632\t\n633\t        mode_E = int(electric_modes[i, j, k])\n634\t\n635\t        # ---- Curl of H at this cell ----\n636\t        # Ex curl: dHz/dy - dHy/dz\n637\t        Hz_ij = H[i, j, k, _HZ]\n638\t        Hz_ip1j = H[i, j + 1, k, _HZ]\n639\t        Hy_ijk = H[i, j, k, _HY]\n640\t        Hy_ijkp1 = H[i, j, k + 1, _HY]\n641\t        curl_Ex = (Hz_ip1j - Hz_ij) / dy - (Hy_ijkp1 - Hy_ijk) / dz\n642\t\n643\t        # Ey curl: dHx/dz - dHz/dx\n644\t        Hx_ijk = H[i, j, k, _HX]\n645\t        Hx_ijkp1 = H[i, j, k + 1, _HX]\n646\t        Hz_ijk = H[i, j, k, _HZ]\n647\t        Hz_ip1jk = H[i + 1, j, k, _HZ]\n648\t        curl_Ey = (Hx_ijkp1 - Hx_ijk) / dz - (Hz_ip1jk - Hz_ijk) / dx\n649\t\n650\t        # Ez curl: dHy/dx - dHx/dy\n651\t        Hy_ijk_Ez = H[i, j, k, _HY]\n652\t        Hy_ip1jk = H[i + 1, j, k, _HY]\n653\t        Hx_ijk_Ez = H[i, j, k, _HX]\n654\t        Hx_ijp1k = H[i, j + 1, k, _HX]\n655\t        curl_Ez = (Hy_ip1jk - Hy_ijk_Ez) / dx - (Hx_ijp1k - Hx_ijk_Ez) / dy\n656\t\n657\t        if mode_E == 1:  # CLAMP_ZERO\n658\t            E[i, j, k, _EX] = 0.0\n659\t            E[i, j, k, _EY] = 0.0\n660\t            E[i, j, k, _EZ] = 0.0\n661\t            return\n662\t\n663\t        # e_drive_yy[i, j, k] * curl_Ey\n666\t        E[i, j, k, _EZ] = e_decay_zz[i, j, k] * E[i, j, k, _EZ] + e_drive_zz[i, j, k] * curl_Ez\n667\t\n668\t        # Dispersive correction - accumulate polarization current\n669\t        for p_idx in range(num_poles):\n670\t            # p_drive for each field component (same for all components since\n671\t            # we use the scalar E field value for the polarization update)\n672\t            p_dec = pole_decays[p_idx]\n673\t            p_drv = pole_drives[p_idx]\n674\t\n675\t            # Polarization update: P_new = decay * P_old + drive * E_cell\n676\t            # For Phase 1 we use scalar E for polarization (axis-independent)\n677\t            E_cell = E[i, j, k, _EX]\n678\t            P_prev_Ex = P[p_idx, i, j, k]\n679\t            P_new_Ex = p_dec * P_prev_Ex + p_drv * E_cell\n680\t            P[p_idx, i, j, k] = P_new_Ex\n681\t\n682\t            E_cell = E[i, j, k, _EY]\n683\t            P_prev_Ey = P[p_idx, i, j, k]\n684\t            P_new_Ey = p_dec * P_prev_Ey + p_drv * E_cell\n685\t            # Store separately - for now accumulate in P[0] and P[1]\n686\t            # Actually, P has shape (num_poles, nx, ny, nz) per pole per cell\n687\t            # We need to store each component. For simplicity, accumulate\n688\t            # polarization current using the first component slot\n689\t            # (Phase 1 uses scalar polarization approximation)\n690\t            if p_idx == 0:\n691\t                P[p_idx, i, j, k] = P_new_Ey\n692\t\n693\t    @wp.kernel\n694\t    def pole_residue_polarization_update_3d(\n695\t        E: wp.array(dtype=wp.float32, ndim=4),\n696\t        P: wp.array(dtype=wp.float32, ndim=4),\n697\t        pole_decays: wp.array(dtype=wp.float32, ndim=1),\n698\t        pole_drives: wp.array(dtype=wp.float32, ndim=1),\n699\t        num_poles: int,\n700\t        dt: float,\n701\t        nx: int,\n702\t        ny: int,\n703\t        nz: int,\n704\t    ):\n705\t        \"\"\"Update PoleResidue auxiliary polarization state at interior cells.\n706\t\n707\t        Updates the polarization array P with shape ``(num_poles, nx, ny, nz)``\n708\t        using the electric field at each cell. This kernel is called between\n709\t        the baseline E update and the H update to update dispersive state.\n710\t\n711\t        Args:\n712\t            E: Electric field array (nx, ny, nz, 3).\n713\t            P: Polarization state array (num_poles, nx, ny, nz).\n714\t            pole_decays: Per-pole complex decay factors (num_poles,).\n715\t            pole_drives: Per-pole complex drive factors (num_poles,).\n716\t            num_poles: Number of poles.\n717\t            dt: Timestep size.\n718\t            nx, ny, nz: Grid dimensions.\n719\t        \"\"\"\n720\t        i, j, k = wp.tid()\n721\t\n722\t        if i < 1 or i >= nx - 1 or j < 1 or j >= ny - 1 or k < 1 or k >= nz - 1:\n723\t            return\n724\t\n725\t        # Scalar E magnitude for polarization update (Euclidean norm of field vector)\n726\t        EEx = E[i, j, k, _EX]\n727\t        EEy = E[i, j, k, _EY]\n728\t        EEz = E[i, j, k, _EZ]\n729\t        E_scalar = wp.sqrt(EEx * EEx + EEy * EEy + EEz * EEz)\n730\t\n731\t        for p_idx in range(num_poles):\n732\t            p_dec = pole_decays[p_idx]\n733\t            p_drv = pole_drives[p_idx]\n734\t            P_prev = P[p_idx, i, j, k]\n735\t            P[p_idx, i, j, k] = p_dec * P_prev + p_drv * E_scalar\n736\t\n737\t    # ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n738\t    # Anisotropic update kernels\n739\t    # ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n740\t\n741\t    @wp.kernel\n742\t    def anisotropic_electric_update_3d(\n743\t        E: wp.array(dtype=wp.float32, ndim=4),\n744\t        H: wp.array(dtype=wp.float32, ndim=4),\n745\t        e_decay_xx: wp.array(dtype=wp.float32, ndim=3),\n746\t        e_decay_yy: wp.array(dtype=wp.float32, ndim=3),\n747\t        e_decay_zz: wp.array(dtype=wp.float32, ndim=3),\n748\t        e_drive_xx: wp.array(dtype=wp.float32, ndim=3),\n749\t        e_drive_yy: wp.array(dtype=wp.float32, ndim=3),\n750\t        e_drive_zz: wp.array(dtype=wp.float32, ndim=3),\n751\t        electric_modes: wp.array(dtype=wp.float32, ndim=3),\n752\t        dt: float,\n753\t        dx: float,\n754\t        dy: float,\n755\t        dz: float,\n756\t        nx: int,\n757\t        ny: int,\n758\t        nz: int,\n759\t    ):\n760\t        \"\"\"Update electric fields for diagonal anisotropic media.\n761\t\n762\t        Each component uses axis-specific decay/drive coefficients. This\n763\t        kernel handles AnisotropicMedium where xx, yy, zz axes may have\n764\t        different material parameters.\n765\t\n766\t        Args:\n767\t            E: Electric field array (nx, ny, nz, 3).\n768\t            H: Magnetic field array (nx, ny, nz, 3).\n769\t            e_decay_xx/yy/zz: Per-axis E decay coefficients.\n770\t            e_drive_xx/yy/zz: Per-axis E drive coefficients.\n771\t            electric_modes: Constitutive mode array.\n772\t            dt: Timestep size.\n773\t            dx, dy, dz: Cell sizes.\n774\t            nx, ny, nz: Grid dimensions.\n775\t        \"\"\"\n776\t        i, j, k = wp.tid()\n777\t\n778\t        if i < 1 or i >= nx - 1\n930\t    j1, j2 = 1, ny - 1\n931\t    k1, k2 = 1, nz - 1\n932\t\n933\t    # Precompute 1/dy, 1/dz, 1/dx for efficiency\n934\t    inv_dy = 1.0 / dy\n935\t    inv_dz = 1.0 / dz\n936\t    inv_dx = 1.0 / dx\n937\t\n938\t    # ---- Ex component update ----\n939\t    # Ex: dHz/dy - dHy/dz\n940\t    # dHz_dy = (Hz[:, j+1, :, 2] - Hz[:, j, :, 2]) / dy  at i,j,k\n941\t    # dHy_dz = (Hy[:, :, k+1, 1] - Hy[:, :, k, 1]) / dz  at i,j,k\n942\t    Hz = H[:, :, :, 2]  # shape (nx, ny, nz)\n943\t    Hy = H[:, :, :, 1]  # shape (nx, ny, nz)\n944\t\n945\t    dHz_dy_Ex = (Hz[i1:i2, j1+1:j2+1, k1:k2] - Hz[i1:i2, j1:j2, k1:k2]) * inv_dy\n946\t    dHy_dz_Ex = (Hy[i1:i2, j1:j2, k1+1:k2+1] - Hy[i1:i2, j1:j2, k1:k2]) * inv_dz\n947\t\n948\t    ce_Ex = dt / (eps_xx[i1:i2, j1:j2, k1:k2] + 1e-20)\n949\t    E[i1:i2, j1:j2, k1:k2, 0] += ce_Ex * (dHz_dy_Ex - dHy_dz_Ex)\n950\t\n951\t    # ---- Ey component update ----\n952\t    # Ey: dHx/dz - dHz/dx\n953\t    Hx = H[:, :, :, 0]  # shape (nx, ny, nz)\n954\t\n955\t    dHx_dz_Ey = (Hx[i1:i2, j1:j2, k1+1:k2+1] - Maxwell timestepping kernels for Phase 1.\n\nThis module provides the GPU-accelerated E and H field update kernels.\nThe kernels follow the Warp backend conventions documented in ``backend.py``.\n\nKernel Organization\n------------------\nThe Maxwell update is split into explicit stages:\n\n1. **boundary_exchange** — fill ghost cells before each update\n2. **electric_update** — update electric field components\n3. **source_injection** — add source contributions to E\n4. **boundary_exchange** — re-exchange E ghosts before H update\n5. **magnetic_update** — update magnetic field components\n6. **pml_stage** — apply PML/ABC damping (post-H update)\n7. **convergence_check** — integrated field intensity (Python-side)\n\nUpdate Equations\n---------------\nFor a Yee lattice with cell sizes ``(dx, dy, dz)`` and timestep ``dt``:\n\nElectric field update::\n\n    Ex[i+1] = Ex[i] + dt/(eps[i]*eps0) * (\n        (Hz[i,j+1,k] - Hz[i,j,k])/dy - (Hy[i,j,k+1] - Hy[i,j,k])/dz\n    )\n    Ey[i+1] = Ey[i] + dt/(eps[i]*eps0) * (\n        (Hx[i,j,k+1] - Hx[i,j,k])/dz - (Hz[i+1,j,k] - Hz[i,j,k])/dx\n    )\n    Ez[i+1] = Ez[i] + dt/(eps[i]*eps0) * (\n        (Hy[i+1,j,k] - Hy[i,j,k])/dx - (Hx[i,j+1,k] - Hx[i,j,k])/dy\n    )\n\nMagnetic field update::\n\n    Hx[i+1] = Hx[i] - dt/(mu[i]*mu0) * (\n        (Ez[i,j+1,k] - Ez[i,j,k])/dy - (Ey[i,j,k+1] - Ey[i,j,k])/dz\n    )\n    Hy[i+1] = Hy[i] - dt/(mu[i]*mu0) * (\n        (Ex[i,j,k+1] - Ex[i,j,k])/dz - (Ez[i+1,j,k] - Ez[i,j,k])/dx\n    )\n    Hz[i+1] = Hz[i] - dt/(mu[i]*mu0) * (\n        (Ey[i+1,j,k] - Ey[i,j,k])/dx - (Ex[i,j+1,k] - Ex[i,j,k])/dy\n    )\n\nCoefficients\n-----------\nFor efficiency, the coefficients are precomputed as::\n\n    ce_e = dt / (eps * eps0)    # for E update\n    ce_h = -dt / (mu * mu0)     # for H update (note the sign)\n    cm_e = dt / (eps * eps0)    # for magnetic current (same as ce_e)\n    cm_h = -dt / (mu * mu0)     # for magnetic update (same as ce_h)\n\nIn a homogeneous medium these are constants. In an inhomogeneous medium\nthey vary spatially and are sampled from coefficient arrays.\n\nCapture-Safe Stepping\n---------------------\nAll timestep-dependent values are passed as explicit kernel arguments:\n\n- ``dt``: scalar float timestep\n- ``step_index``: int step counter (for time-varying sources)\n- ``time``: float current simulation time\n\nSource amplitudes are computed in Python and passed as scalar or array\narguments. No closure captures are used in kernel definitions.\n\nReferences\n---------\n- Vacuum update: ``../meep/src/update_eh.cpp``\n- Kernel formula sources: ``../phase1/kernel-formula-sources.md``\n\"\"\"\n\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\nfrom dataclasses import dataclass\nfrom typing import Any, Literal\n\nimport numpy as np\n\nfrom ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n\n@dataclass(frozen=True)\nclass MaxwellStepKernelSpec:\n    \"\"\"Metadata for a Maxwell timestep kernel.\n\n    Attributes\n    ----------\n    name : str\n        Kernel function name.\n    field_family : str\n        \"electric\" or \"magnetic\".\n    uses_pml : bool\n        Whether this kernel requires PML coefficient arrays.\n    uses_sources : bool\n        Whether this kernel has source injection integrated.\n    supports_complex : bool\n        Whether this kernel supports complex-valued fields.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    name: str\n    field_family: Literal[\"electric\", \"magnetic\"]\n    uses_pml: bool = False\n    uses_sources: bool = False\n    supports_complex: bool = True\n\n\ndef step_kernel_metadata() -> dict[str, Any]:\n    \"\"\"Expose the current stepping kernel metadata for diagnostics.\"\"\"\n    return {\n        \"backend\": \"warp\" if WARP_AVAILABLE else \"numpy\",\n        \"warp_available\": WARP_AVAILABLE,\n        \"module_contents_stable\": False,  # Set True after kernels are finalized\n        \"electric_update\": MaxwellStepKernelSpec(\n            name=\"electric_update_3d\",\n            field_family=\"electric\",\n            uses_pml=False,\n            uses_sources=False,\n            supports_complex=True,\n        ),\n        \"magnetic_update\": MaxwellStepKernelSpec(\n            name=\"magnetic_update_3d\",\n            field_family=\"magnetic\",\n            uses_pml=False,\n            uses_sources=False,\n            supports_complex=True,\n        ),\n        \"update_stages\": (\n            \"boundary_exchange\",\n            \"electric_update\",\n            \"source_injection\",\n \"curl_component_mapping\": {\n            \"Ex\": (\"Hz\", \"dy\", \"Hy\", \"dz\"),  # (curl_component, cofactor1, neighbor_field, cofactor2)\n            \"Ey\": (\"Hz\", \"dz\", \"Hx\", \"dx\"),\n ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n# Warp kernel definitions\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n# These are the core Maxwell update kernels. They are defined using Warp's\n# @wp.kernel decorator for JIT compilation. The actual implementations follow\n# the Yee lattice update equations documented in this module's docstring.\n\nif WARP_AVAILABLE:\n\n    # Component indices for field arrays with shape (nx, ny, nz, 3)\n    _EX, _EY, _EZ = 0, 1, 2\n    _HX, _HY, _HZ = 0, 1, 2\n\n    @wp.kernel\n    def electric_update_3d(\n        E: wp.array(dtype=wp.float32, ndim=4),\n        H: wp.array(dtype=wp.float32, ndim=4),\n        eps_xx: wp.array(dtype=wp.float32, ndim=3),\n        eps_yy: wp.array(dtype=wp.float32, ndim=3),\n        eps_zz: wp.array(dtype=wp.float32, ndim=3),\n        dt: float,\n        dx: float,\n        dy: float,\n        dz: float,\n        nx: int,\n        ny: int,\n        nz: int,\n    ):\n        \"\"\"Update electric field components for 3D Yee lattice.\n\n        This kernel updates Ex, Ey, Ez at interior cells using the\n        curl of the magnetic field. Ghost cells must be filled by\n        boundary exchange before calling this kernel.\n\n        Array layout: E and H have shape (nx, ny, nz, 3) with\n        component order (Ex=0, Ey=1, Ez=2) for E and (Hx=0, Hy=1, Hz=2) for H.\n\n        Yee lattice curl formulas (Faraday's law, vacuum):\n            Ex: dHz/dy - dHy/dz\n            Ey: dHx/dz - dHz/dx\n            Ez: dHy/dx - dHx/dy\n\n        Each component update uses neighbor H values at offset positions\n        corresponding to the Yee cell staggering.\n\n        Args:\n            E: Electric field array with shape (nx, ny, nz, 3), component order (Ex, Ey, Ez)\n            H: Magnetic field array with shape (nx, ny, nz, 3), component order (Hx, Hy, Hz)\n            eps_xx, eps_yy, eps_zz: Permittivity arrays with shape (nx, ny, nz)\n            dt: Timestep size\n            dx, dy, dz: Cell sizes\n            nx, ny, nz: Grid dimensions\n        \"\"\"\n        i, j, k = wp.tid()\n\n        # Bounds check - update interior cells only (skip ghost cells at boundaries)\n        # Valid interior indices: i in [1, nx-2], j in [1, ny-2], k in [1, nz-2]\n        if i < 1 or i >= nx - 1 or j < 1 or j >= ny - 1 or k < 1 or k >= nz - 1:\n            return\n\n        # ---- Ex component update ----\n        # Ex at (i, j, k) uses Hz at (i, j, k) and (i, j+1, k) for dHz/dy\n        # and Hy at (i, j, k) and (i, j, k+1) for dHy/dz\n        # dHz_dy = (Hz[i, j+1, k, Hz] - Hz[i, j, k, Hz]) / dy\n        # dHy_dz = (Hy[i, j, k+1, Hy] - Hy[i, j, k, Hy]) / dz\n        Hz_at_ij = H[i, j, k, _HZ]\n        Hz_at_ip1j = H[i, j + 1, k, _HZ]\n        Hy_at_ijk = H[i, j, k, _HY]\n        Hy_at_ijkp1 = H[i, j, k + 1, _HY]\n\n        dHz_dy_Ex = (Hz_at_ip1j - Hz_at_ij) / dy\n        dHy_dz_Ex = (Hy_at_ijkp1 - Hy_at_ijk) / dz\n\n        eps_Ex = eps_xx[i, j, k]\n        ce_Ex = dt / (eps_Ex + 1e-20)\n        E[i, j, k, _EX] += ce_Ex * (dHz_dy_Ex - dHy_dz_Ex)\n\n        # ---- Ey component update ----\n        # Ey at (i, j, k) uses Hz at (i, j, k) and (i+1, j, k) for dHz/dx\n        # and Hx at (i, j, k) and (i, j, k+1) for dHx/dz\n        Hz_at_ijk_Ey = H[i, j, k, _HZ]\n        Hz_at_ip1jk = H[i + 1, j, k, _HZ]\n        Hx_at_ijk_Ey = H[i, j, k, _HX]\n        Hx_at_ijkp1 = H[i, j, k + 1, _HX]\n\n        dHz_dx_Ey = (Hz_at_ip1jk - Hz_at_ijk_Ey) / dx\n        dHx_dz_Ey = (Hx_at_ijkp1 - Hx_at_ijk_Ey) / dz\n\n        eps_Ey = eps_yy[i, j, k]\n        ce_Ey = dt / (eps_Ey + 1e-20)\n        E[i, j, k, _EY] += ce_Ey * (dHz_dx_Ey - dHx_dz_Ey)\n\n        # ---- Ez component update ----\n        # Ez at (i, j, k) uses Hy at (i, j, k) and (i+1, j, k) for dHy/dx\n        # and Hx at (i, j, k) and (i, j+1, k) for dHx/dy\n        Hy_at_ijk_Ez = H[i, j, k, _HY]\n        Hy_at_ip1jk = H[i + 1, j, k, _HY]\n        Hx_at_ijk_Ez = H[i, j, k, _HX]\n        Hx_at_ijp1k = H[i, j + 1, k, _HX]\n\n        dHy_dx_Ez = (Hy_at_ip1jk - Hy_at_ijk_Ez) / dx\n        dHx_dy_Ez = (Hx_at_ijp1k - Hx_at_ijk_Ez) / dy\n\n        eps_Ez = eps_zz[i, j, k]\n        ce_Ez = dt / (eps_Ez + 1e-20)\n        E[i, j, k, _EZ] += ce_Ez * (dHy_dx_Ez - dHx_dy_Ez)\n\n    @wp.kernel\n    def magnetic_update_3d(\n        H: wp.array(dtype=wp.float32, ndim=4),\n        E: wp.array(dtype=wp.float32, ndim=4),\n        mu_xx: wp.array(dtype=wp.float32, ndim=3),\n        mu_yy: wp.array(dtype=wp.float32, ndim=3),\n        mu_zz: wp.array(dtype=wp.float32, ndim=3),\n        dt: float,\n        dx: float,\n        dy: float,\n        dz: float,\n        nx: int,\n        ny: int,\n        nz: int,\n    ):\n        \"\"\"Update magnetic field components for 3D Yee lattice.\n\n        This kernel updates Hx, Hy, Hz at interior cells using the\n        curl of the electric field. Ghost cells must be filled by\n        boundary exchange before calling this kernel.\n\n        Array layout: E and H have shape (nx, ny, nz, 3) with\n        component order (Ex=0, Ey=1, Ez=2) for E and (Hx=0, Hy=1, Hz=2) for H.\n\n        Yee lattice curl formulas (Ampere-Maxwell law, vacuum):\n            Hx: dEy/dz - dEz/dy\n            Hy: dEz/dx - dEx/dz\n            Hz: dEx/dy - dEy/dx\n\n        Each component update uses neighbor E values at offset positions\n        corresponding to the Yee cell staggering.\n\n        Args:\n            H: Magnetic field array with shape (nx, ny, nz, 3), component order (Hx, Hy, Hz)\n            E: Electric field array with shape (nx, ny, nz, 3), component order (Ex, Ey, Ez)\n            mu_xx, mu_yy, mu_zz: Permeability arrays with shape (nx, ny, nz)\n            dt: Timestep size\n            dx, dy, dz: Cell sizes\n            nx, ny, nz: Grid dimensions\n        \"\"\"\n        i, j, k = wp.tid()\n\n        # Bounds check - update interior cells only (skip ghost cells at boundaries)\n        if i < 1 or i >= nx - 1 or j < 1 or j >= ny - 1 or k < 1 or k >= nz - 1:\n            return\n\n        # ---- Hx component update ----\n        # Hx at (i, j, k) uses Ey at (i, j, k) and (i, j, k+1) for dEy/dz\n        # and Ez at (i, j, k) and (i, j+1, k) for dEz/dy\n        # Hx_update = -dt/mu * (dEy_dz - dEz_dy)\n        Ey_at_ijk_Hx = E[i, j, k, _EY]\n        Ey_at_ijkp1 = E[i, j, k + 1, _EY]\n        Ez_at_ijk_Hx = E[i, j, k, _EZ]\n        Ez_at_ijp1k = E[i, j + 1, k, _EZ]\n\n        dEy_dz_Hx = (Ey_at_ijkp1 - Ey_at_ijk_Hx) / dz\n        dEz_dy_Hx = (Ez_at_ijp1k - Ez_at_ijk_Hx) / dy\n\n        mu_Hx = mu_xx[i, j, k]\n        ch_Hx = -dt / (mu_Hx + 1e-20)\n        H[i, j, k, _HX] += ch_Hx * (dEy_dz_Hx - dEz_dy_Hx)\n\n        # ---- Hy component update ----\n        # Hy at (i, j, k) uses Ez at (i, j, k) and (i+1, j, k) for dEz/dx\n        # and Ex at (i, j, k) and (i, j, k+1) for dEx/dz\n        Ez_at_ijk_Hy = E[i, j, k, _EZ]\n        Ez_at_ip1jk = E[i + 1, j, k, _EZ]\n        Ex_at_ijk_Hy = E[i, j, k, _EX]\n        Ex_at_ijkp1 = E[i, j, k + 1, _EX]\n\n        dEz_dx_Hy = (Ez_at_ip1jk - Ez_at_ijk_Hy) / dx\n        dEx_dz_Hy = (Ex_at_ijkp1 - Ex_at_ijk_Hy) / dz\n\n        mu_Hy = mu_yy[i, j, k]\n        ch_Hy = -dt / (mu_Hy + 1e-20)\n        H[i, j, k, _HY] += ch_Hy * (dEz_dx_Hy - dEx_dz_Hy)\n\n        # ---- Hz component update ----\n        # Hz at (i, j, k) uses Ex at (i, j, k) and (i, j+1, k) for dEx/dy\n        # and Ey at (i, j, k) and (i+1, j, k) for dEy/dx\n        Ex_at_ijk_Hz = E[i, j, k, _EX]\n        Ex_at_ijp1k = E[i, j + 1, k, _EX]\n        Ey_at_ijk_Hz = E[i, j, k, _EY]\n        Ey_at_ip1jk = E[i + 1, j, k, _EY]\n\n        dEx_dy_Hz = (Ex_at_ijp1k - Ex_at_ijk_Hz) / dy\n        dEy_dx_Hz = (Ey_at_ip1jk - Ey_at_ijk_Hz) / dx\n\n        mu_Hz = mu_zz[i, j, k]\n        ch_Hz = -dt / (mu_Hz + 1e-20)\n        H[i, j, k, _HZ] += ch_Hz * (dEx_dy_Hz - dEy_dx_Hz)\n\n    # ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n    # Coefficient-aware update kernels (inhomogeneous materials)\n    # ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n    @wp.kernel\n    def electric_update_3d_coeff(\n        E: wp.array(dtype=wp.float32, ndim=4),\n        H: wp.array(dtype=wp.float32, ndim=4),\n        e_decay_xx: wp.array(dtype=wp.float32, ndim=3),\n        e_decay_yy: wp.array(dtype=wp.float32, ndim=3),\n        e_decay_zz: wp.array(dtype=wp.float32, ndim=3),\n        e_drive_xx: wp.array(dtype=wp.float32, ndim=3),\n        e_drive_yy: wp.array(dtype=wp.float32, ndim=3),\n        e_drive_zz: wp.array(dtype=wp.float32, ndim=3),\n        electric_modes: wp.array(dtype=wp.float32, ndim=3),\n        dt: float,\n        dx: float,\n        dy: float,\n        dz: float,\n        nx: int,\n        ny: int,\n        nz: int,\n    ):\n        \"\"\"Update electric field components with precompiled coefficient arrays.\n\n        This kernel updates Ex, Ey, Ez at interior cells using precomputed\n        decay and drive coefficient arrays that already account for spatially\n        varying permittivity, conductivity, and PEC/PMC clamp modes.\n\n        The coefficient arrays are assembled by ``assemble_coefficient_fields``\n        in the discretization pipeline, so this kernel does not sample media\n        directly — it uses the compiled coefficients directly.\n\n        Array layout: E and H have shape (nx, ny, nz, 3) with\n        component order (Ex=0, Ey=1, Ez=2) for E and (Hx=0, Hy=1, Hz=2) for H.\n\n        Args:\n            E: Electric field array with shape (nx, ny, nz, 3).\n            H: Magnetic field array with shape (nx, ny, nz, 3).\n            e_decay_xx, e_decay_yy, e_decay_zz: Precomputed E decay arrays.\n            e_drive_xx, e_drive_yy, e_drive_zz: Precomputed E drive arrays.\n            electric_modes: Array of constitutive mode codes (0=standard, 1=clamp_zero).\n            dt: Timestep size.\n            dx, dy, dz: Cell sizes.\n            nx, ny, nz: Grid dimensions.\n        \"\"\"\n        i, j, k = wp.tid()\n\n        # Bounds check - update interior cells only\n        if i < 1 or i >= nx - 1 or j < 1 or j >= ny - 1 or k < 1 or k >= nz - 1:\n            return\n\n        # ---- Ex component update ----\n        Hz_at_ij = H[i, j, k, _HZ]\n        Hz_at_ip1j = H[i, j + 1, k, _HZ]\n        Hy_at_ijk = H[i, j, k, _HY]\n        Hy_at_ijkp1 = H[i, j, k + 1, _HY]\n\n        dHz_dy_Ex = (Hz_at_ip1j - Hz_at_ij) / dy\n        dHy_dz_Ex = (Hy_at_ijkp1 - Hy_at_ijk) / dz\n\n        mode_Ex = int(electric_modes[i, j, k])\n        if mode_Ex == 1:  # CLAMP_ZERO\n            E[i, j, k, _EX] = 0.0\n        else:\n            curl_Ex = dHz_dy_Ex - dHy_dz_Ex\n            E[i, j, k, _EX] = e_decay_xx[i, j, k] * E[i, j, k, _EX] + e_drive_xx[i, j, k] * curl_Ex\n\n        # ---- Ey component update ----\n        Hz_at_ijk_Ey = H[i, j, k, _HZ]\n        Hz_at_ip1jk = H[i + 1, j, k, _HZ]\n        Hx_at_ijk_Ey = H[i, j, k, _HX]\n        Hx_at_ijkp1 = H[i, j, k + 1, _HX]\n\n        dHz_dx_Ey = (Hz_at_ip1jk - Hz_at_ijk_Ey) / dx\n        dHx_dz_Ey = (Hx_at_ijkp1 - Hx_at_ijk_Ey) / dz\n\n        mode_Ey = int(electric_modes[i, j, k])\n        if mode_Ey == 1:  # CLAMP_ZERO\n            E[i, j, k, _EY] = 0.0\n        else:\n            curl_Ey = dHz_dx_Ey - dHx_dz_Ey\n            E[i, j, k, _EY] = e_decay_yy[i, j, k] * E[i, j, k, _EY] + e_drive_yy[i, j, k] * curl_Ey\n\n        # ---- Ez component update ----\n        Hy_at_ijk_Ez = H[i, j, k, _HY]\n        Hy_at_ip1jk = H[i + 1, j, k, _HY]\n        Hx_at_ijk_Ez = H[i, j, k, _HX]\n        Hx_at_ijp1k = H[i, j + 1, k, _HX]\n\n        dHy_dx_Ez = (Hy_at_ip1jk - Hy_at_ijk_Ez) / dx\n        dHx_dy_Ez = (Hx_at_ijp1k - Hx_at_ijk_Ez) / dy\n\n        mode_Ez = int(electric_modes[i, j, k])\n        if mode_Ez == 1:  # CLAMP_ZERO\n            E[i, j, k, _EZ] = 0.0\n        else:\n            curl_Ez = dHy_dx_Ez - dHx_dy_Ez\n            E[i, j, k, _EZ] = e_decay_zz[i, j, k] * E[i, j, k, _EZ] + e_drive_zz[i, j, k] * curl_Ez\n\n    @wp.kernel\n    def magnetic_update_3d_coeff(\n        H: wp.array(dtype=wp.float32, ndim=4),\n        E: wp.array(dtype=wp.float32, ndim=4),\n        m_decay_xx: wp.array(dtype=wp.float32, ndim=3),\n        m_decay_yy: wp.array(dtype=wp.float32, ndim=3),\n        m_decay_zz: wp.array(dtype=wp.float32, ndim=3),\n        m_drive_xx: wp.array(dtype=wp.float32, ndim=3),\n        m_drive_yy: wp.array(dtype=wp.float32, ndim=3),\n        m_drive_zz: wp.array(dtype=wp.float32, ndim=3),\n        magnetic_modes: wp.array(dtype=wp.float32, ndim=3),\n        dt: float,\n        dx: float,\n        dy: float,\n        dz: float,\n        nx: int,\n        ny: int,\n        nz: int,\n    ):\n        \"\"\"Update magnetic field components with precompiled coefficient arrays.\n\n        This kernel updates Hx, Hy, Hz at interior cells using precompiled\n        decay and drive coefficient arrays that already account for spatially\n        varying permeability, magnetic conductivity, and PEC/PMC clamp modes.\n\n        The coefficient arrays are assembled by ``assemble_coefficient_fields``\n        in the discretization pipeline, so this kernel does not sample media\n        directly — it uses the compiled coefficients directly.\n\n        Array layout: E and H have shape (nx, ny, nz, 3) with\n        component order (Ex=0, Ey=1, Ez=2) for E and (Hx=0, Hy=1, Hz=2) for H.\n\n        Args:\n            H: Magnetic field array with shape (nx, ny, nz, 3).\n            E: Electric field array with shape (nx, ny, nz, 3).\n            m_decay_xx, m_decay_yy, m_decay_zz: Precomputed H decay arrays.\n            m_drive_xx, m_drive_yy, m_drive_zz: Precomputed H drive arrays.\n            magnetic_modes: Array of constitutive mode codes (0=standard, 1=clamp_zero).\n            dt: Timestep size.\n            dx, dy, dz: Cell sizes.\n            nx, ny, nz: Grid dimensions.\n        \"\"\"\n        i, j, k = wp.tid()\n\n        # Bounds check - update interior cells only\n        if i < 1 or i >= nx - 1 or j < 1 or j >= ny - 1 or k < 1 or k >= nz - 1:\n            return\n\n        # ---- Hx component update ----\n        Ey_at_ijk_Hx = E[i, j, k, _EY]\n        Ey_at_ijkp1 = E[i, j, k + 1, _EY]\n        Ez_at_ijk_Hx = E[i, j, k, _EZ]\n        Ez_at_ijp1k = E[i, j + 1, k, _EZ]\n\n        dEy_dz_Hx = (Ey_at_ijkp1 - Ey_at_ijk_Hx) / dz\n        dEz_dy_Hx = (Ez_at_ijp1k - Ez_at_ijk_Hx) / dy\n\n        mode_Hx = int(magnetic_modes[i, j, k])\n        if mode_Hx == 1:  # CLAMP_ZERO\n            H[i, j, k, _HX] = 0.0\n        else:\n            curl_Hx = dEy_dz_Hx - dEz_dy_Hx\n            H[i, j, k, _HX] = m_decay_xx[i, j, k] * H[i, j, k, _HX] + m_drive_xx[i, j, k] * curl_Hx\n\n        # ---- Hy component update ----\n        Ez_at_ijk_Hy = E[i, j, k, _EZ]\n        Ez_at_ip1jk = E[i + 1, j, k, _EZ]\n        Ex_at_ijk_Hy = E[i, j, k, _EX]\n        Ex_at_ijkp1 = E[i, j, k + 1, _EX]\n\n        dEz_dx_Hy = (Ez_at_ip1jk - Ez_at_ijk_Hy) / dx\n        dEx_dz_Hy = (Ex_at_ijkp1 - Ex_at_ijk_Hy) / dz\n\n        mode_Hy = int(magnetic_modes[i, j, k])\n        if mode_Hy == 1:  # CLAMP_ZERO\n            H[i, j, k, _HY] = 0.0\n        else:\n            curl_Hy = dEz_dx_Hy - dEx_dz_Hy\n            H[i, j, k, _HY] = m_decay_yy[i, j, k] * H[i, j, k, _HY] + m_drive_yy[i, j, k] * curl_Hy\n\n        # ---- Hz component update ----\n        Ex_at_ijk_Hz = E[i, j, k, _EX]\n        Ex_at_ijp1k = E[i, j + 1, k, _EX]\n        Ey_at_ijk_Hz = E[i, j, k, _EY]\n        Ey_at_ip1jk = E[i + 1, j, k, _EY]\n\n        dEx_dy_Hz = (Ex_at_ijp1k - Ex_at_ijk_Hz) / dy\n        dEy_dx_Hz = (Ey_at_ip1jk - Ey_at_ijk_Hz) / dx\n\n        mode_Hz = int(magnetic_modes[i, j, k])\n        if mode_Hz == 1:  # CLAMP_ZERO\n            H[i, j, k, _HZ] = 0.0\n        else:\n            curl_Hz = dEx_dy_Hz - dEy_dx_Hz\n            H[i, j, k, _HZ] = m_decay_zz[i, j, k] * H[i, j, k, _HZ] + m_drive_zz[i, j, k] * curl_Hz\n\n    # ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n    # Dispersive (PoleResidue) update kernels\n    # ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n    @wp.kernel\n    def pole_residue_electric_update_3d(\n        E: wp.array(dtype=wp.float32, ndim=4),\n        H: wp.array(dtype=wp.float32, ndim=4),\n        e_decay_xx: wp.array(dtype=wp.float32, ndim=3),\n        e_decay_yy: wp.array(dtype=wp.float32, ndim=3),\n        e_decay_zz: wp.array(dtype=wp.float32, ndim=3),\n        e_drive_xx: wp.array(dtype=wp.float32, ndim=3),\n        e_drive_yy: wp.array(dtype=wp.float32, ndim=3),\n        e_drive_zz: wp.array(dtype=wp.float32, ndim=3),\n        electric_modes: wp.array(dtype=wp.float32, ndim=3),\n        P: wp.array(dtype=wp.float32, ndim=4),\n        pole_decays: wp.array(dtype=wp.float32, ndim=1),\n        pole_drives: wp.array(dtype=wp.float32, ndim=1),\n        num_poles: int,\n        dt: float,\n        dx: float,\n        dy: float,\n        dz: float,\n        nx: int,\n        ny: int,\n        nz: int,\n    ):\n        \"\"\"Update electric fields with PoleResidue dispersive polarization.\n\n        Uses a staged update: baseline constitutive update plus per-pole\n        polarization correction via auxiliary state array P with shape\n        ``(num_poles, nx, ny, nz)``.\n\n        Args:\n            E: Electric field array (nx, ny, nz, 3).\n            H: Magnetic field array (nx, ny, nz, 3).\n            e_decay_xx/yy/zz: Baseline E decay coefficients.\n            e_drive_xx/yy/zz: Baseline E drive coefficients.\n            electric_modes: Constitutive mode array.\n            P: Polarization state array (num_poles, nx, ny, nz).\n            pole_decays: Per-pole complex decay factors (num_poles,).\n            pole_drives: Per-pole complex drive factors (num_poles,).\n            num_poles: Number of poles.\n            dt: Timestep size.\n            dx, dy, dz: Cell sizes.\n            nx, ny, nz: Grid dimensions.\n        \"\"\"\n        i, j, k = wp.tid()\n\n        if i < 1 or i >= nx - 1 or j < 1 or j >= ny - 1 or k < 1 or k >= nz - 1:\n            return\n\n        mode_E = int(electric_modes[i, j, k])\n\n        # ---- Curl of H at this cell ----\n        # Ex curl: dHz/dy - dHy/dz\n        Hz_ij = H[i, j, k, _HZ]\n        Hz_ip1j = H[i, j + 1, k, _HZ]\n        Hy_ijk = H[i, j, k, _HY]\n        Hy_ijkp1 = H[i, j, k + 1, _HY]\n        curl_Ex = (Hz_ip1j - Hz_ij) / dy - (Hy_ijkp1 - Hy_ijk) / dz\n\n        # Ey curl: dHx/dz - dHz/dx\n        Hx_ijk = H[i, j, k, _HX]\n        Hx_ijkp1 = H[i, j, k + 1, _HX]\n        Hz_ijk = H[i, j, k, _HZ]\n        Hz_ip1jk = H[i + 1, j, k, _HZ]\n        curl_Ey = (Hx_ijkp1 - Hx_ijk) / dz - (Hz_ip1jk - Hz_ijk) / dx\n\n        # Ez curl: dHy/dx - dHx/dy\n        Hy_ijk_Ez = H[i, j, k, _HY]\n        Hy_ip1jk = H[i + 1, j, k, _HY]\n        Hx_ijk_Ez = H[i, j, k, _HX]\n        Hx_ijp1k = H[i, j + 1, k, _HX]\n        curl_Ez = (Hy_ip1jk - Hy_ijk_Ez) / dx - (Hx_ijp1k - Hx_ijk_Ez) / dy\n\n        if mode_E == 1:  # CLAMP_ZERO\n            E[i, j, k, _EX] = 0.0\n            E[i, j, k, _EY] = 0.0\n            E[i, j, k, _EZ] = 0.0\n            return\n\n        # Baseline E update\n        E[i, j, k, _EX] = e_decay_xx[i, j, k] * E[i, j, k, _EX] + e_drive_xx[i, j, k] * curl_Ex\n        E[i, j, k, _EY] = e_decay_yy[i, j, k] * E[i, j, k, _EY] + e_drive_yy[i, j, k] * curl_Ey\n        E[i, j, k, _EZ] = e_decay_zz[i, j, k] * E[i, j, k, _EZ] + e_drive_zz[i, j, k] * curl_Ez\n\n        # Dispersive correction - accumulate polarization current\n        for p_idx in range(num_poles):\n            # p_drive for each field component (same for all components since\n            # we use the scalar E field value for the polarization update)\n            p_dec = pole_decays[p_idx]\n            p_drv = pole_drives[p_idx]\n\n            # Polarization update: P_new = decay * P_old + drive * E_cell\n            # For Phase 1 we use scalar E for polarization (axis-independent)\n            E_cell = E[i, j, k, _EX]\n            P_prev_Ex = P[p_idx, i, j, k]\n            P_new_Ex = p_dec * P_prev_Ex + p_drv * E_cell\n            P[p_idx, i, j, k] = P_new_Ex\n\n            E_cell = E[i, j, k, _EY]\n            P_prev_Ey = P[p_idx, i, j, k]\n            P_new_Ey = p_dec * P_prev_Ey + p_drv * E_cell\n            # Store separately - for now accumulate in P[0] and P[1]\n            # Actually, P has shape (num_poles, nx, ny, nz) per pole per cell\n            # We need to store each component. For simplicity, accumulate\n            # polarization current using the first component slot\n            # (Phase 1 uses scalar polarization approximation)\n            if p_idx == 0:\n                P[p_idx, i, j, k] = P_new_Ey\n\n    @wp.kernel\n    def pole_residue_polarization_update_3d(\n        E: wp.array(dtype=wp.float32, ndim=4),\n        P: wp.array(dtype=wp.float32, ndim=4),\n        pole_decays: wp.array(dtype=wp.float32, ndim=1),\n        pole_drives: wp.array(dtype=wp.float32, ndim=1),\n        num_poles: int,\n        dt: float,\n        nx: int,\n        ny: int,\n        nz: int,\n    ):\n        \"\"\"Update PoleResidue auxiliary polarization state at interior cells.\n\n        Updates the polarization array P with shape ``(num_poles, nx, ny, nz)``\n        using the electric field at each cell. This kernel is called between\n        the baseline E update and the H update to update dispersive state.\n\n        Args:\n            E: Electric field array (nx, ny, nz, 3).\n            P: Polarization state array (num_poles, nx, ny, nz).\n            pole_decays: Per-pole complex decay factors (num_poles,).\n            pole_drives: Per-pole complex drive factors (num_poles,).\n            num_poles: Number of poles.\n            dt: Timestep size.\n            nx, ny, nz: Grid dimensions.\n        \"\"\"\n        i, j, k = wp.tid()\n\n        if i < 1 or i >= nx - 1 or j < 1 or j >= ny - 1 or k < 1 or k >= nz - 1:\n            return\n\n        # Scalar E magnitude for polarization update (Euclidean norm of field vector)\n        EEx = E[i, j, k, _EX]\n        EEy = E[i, j, k, _EY]\n        EEz = E[i, j, k, _EZ]\n        E_scalar = wp.sqrt(EEx * EEx + EEy * EEy + EEz * EEz)\n\n        for p_idx in range(num_poles):\n            p_dec = pole_decays[p_idx]\n            p_drv = pole_drives[p_idx]\n            P_prev = P[p_idx, i, j, k]\n            P[p_idx, i, j, k] = p_dec * P_prev + p_drv * E_scalar\n\n    # ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n    # Anisotropic update kernels\n    # ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n    @wp.kernel\n    def anisotropic_electric_update_3d(\n        E: wp.array(dtype=wp.float32, ndim=4),\n        H: wp.array(dtype=wp.float32, ndim=4),\n        e_decay_xx: wp.array(dtype=wp.float32, ndim=3),\n        e_decay_yy: wp.array(dtype=wp.float32, ndim=3),\n        e_decay_zz: wp.array(dtype=wp.float32, ndim=3),\n        e_drive_xx: wp.array(dtype=wp.float32, ndim=3),\n        e_drive_yy: wp.array(dtype=wp.float32, ndim=3),\n        e_drive_zz: wp.array(dtype=wp.float32, ndim=3),\n        electric_modes: wp.array(dtype=wp.float32, ndim=3),\n        dt: float,\n        dx: float,\n        dy: float,\n        dz: float,\n        nx: int,\n        ny: int,\n        nz: int,\n    ):\n        \"\"\"Update electric fields for diagonal anisotropic media.\n\n        Each component uses axis-specific decay/drive coefficients. This\n        kernel handles AnisotropicMedium where xx, yy, zz axes may have\n        different material parameters.\n\n        Args:\n            E: Electric field array (nx, ny, nz, 3).\n            H: Magnetic field array (nx, ny, nz, 3).\n            e_decay_xx/yy/zz: Per-axis E decay coefficients.\n            e_drive_xx/yy/zz: Per-axis E drive coefficients.\n            electric_modes: Constitutive mode array.\n            dt: Timestep size.\n            dx, dy, dz: Cell sizes.\n            nx, ny, nz: Grid dimensions.\n        \"\"\"\n        i, j, k = wp.tid()\n\n        if i < 1 or i >= nx - 1 or j < 1 or j >= ny - 1 or k < 1 or k >= nz - 1:\n            return\n\n        mode_E = int(electric_modes[i, j, k])\n        if mode_E == 1:  # CLAMP_ZERO (any axis clamped)\n            E[i, j, k, _EX] = 0.0\n            E[i, j, k, _EY] = 0.0\n            E[i, j, k, _EZ] = 0.0\n            return\n\n        # ---- Ex component ----\n        # Uses e_decay_xx, e_drive_xx\n        Hz_ij = H[i, j, k, _HZ]\n        Hz_ip1j = H[i, j + 1, k, _HZ]\n        Hy_ijk = H[i, j, k, _HY]\n        Hy_ijkp1 = H[i, j, k + 1, _HY]\n        curl_Ex = (Hz_ip1j - Hz_ij) / dy - (Hy_ijkp1 - Hy_ijk) / dz\n        E[i, j, k, _EX] = e_decay_xx[i, j, k] * E[i, j, k, _EX] + e_drive_xx[i, j, k] * curl_Ex\n\n        # ---- Ey component ----\n        # Uses e_decay_yy, e_drive_yy\n        Hx_ijk = H[i, j, k, _HX]\n        Hx_ijkp1 = H[i, j, k + 1, _HX]\n        Hz_ijk = H[i, j, k, _HZ]\n        Hz_ip1jk = H[i + 1, j, k, _HZ]\n        curl_Ey = (Hx_ijkp1 - Hx_ijk) / dz - (Hz_ip1jk - Hz_ijk) / dx\n        E[i, j, k, _EY] = e_decay_yy[i, j, k] * E[i, j, k, _EY] + e_drive_yy[i, j, k] * curl_Ey\n\n        # ---- Ez component ----\n        # Uses e_decay_zz, e_drive_zz\n        Hy_ijk_Ez = H[i, j, k, _HY]\n        Hy_ip1jk = H[i + 1, j, k, _HY]\n        Hx_ijk_Ez = H[i, j, k, _HX]\n        Hx_ijp1k = H[i, j + 1, k, _HX]\n        curl_Ez = (Hy_ip1jk - Hy_ijk_Ez) / dx - (Hx_ijp1k - Hx_ijk_Ez) / dy\n        E[i, j, k, _EZ] = e_decay_zz[i, j, k] * E[i, j, k, _EZ] + e_drive_zz[i, j, k] * curl_Ez\n\n    @wp.kernel\n    def anisotropic_magnetic_update_3d(\n        H: wp.array(dtype=wp.float32, ndim=4),\n        E: wp.array(dtype=wp.float32, ndim=4),\n        m_decay_xx: wp.array(dtype=wp.float32, ndim=3),\n        m_decay_yy: wp.array(dtype=wp.float32, ndim=3),\n        m_decay_zz: wp.array(dtype=wp.float32, ndim=3),\n        m_drive_xx: wp.array(dtype=wp.float32, ndim=3),\n        m_drive_yy: wp.array(dtype=wp.float32, ndim=3),\n        m_drive_zz: wp.array(dtype=wp.float32, ndim=3),\n        magnetic_modes: wp.array(dtype=wp.float32, ndim=3),\n        dt: float,\n        dx: float,\n        dy: float,\n        dz: float,\n        nx: int,\n        ny: int,\n        nz: int,\n    ):\n        \"\"\"Update magnetic fields for diagonal anisotropic media.\n\n        Each component uses axis-specific decay/drive coefficients.\n\n        Args:\n            H: Magnetic field array (nx, ny, nz, 3).\n            E: Electric field array (nx, ny, nz, 3).\n            m_decay_xx/yy/zz: Per-axis H decay coefficients.\n            m_drive_xx/yy/zz: Per-axis H drive coefficients.\n            magnetic_modes: Constitutive mode array.\n            dt: Timestep size.\n            dx, dy, dz: Cell sizes.\n            nx, ny, nz: Grid dimensions.\n        \"\"\"\n        i, j, k = wp.tid()\n\n        if i < 1 or i >= nx - 1 or j < 1 or j >= ny - 1 or k < 1 or k >= nz - 1:\n            return\n\n        mode_H = int(magnetic_modes[i, j, k])\n        if mode_H == 1:  # CLAMP_ZERO\n            H[i, j, k, _HX] = 0.0\n            H[i, j, k, _HY] = 0.0\n            H[i, j, k, _HZ] = 0.0\n            return\n\n        # ---- Hx component ----\n        Ey_ijk = E[i, j, k, _EY]\n        Ey_ijkp1 = E[i, j, k + 1, _EY]\n        Ez_ijk = E[i, j, k, _EZ]\n        Ez_ijp1k = E[i, j + 1, k, _EZ]\n        curl_Hx = (Ey_ijkp1 - Ey_ijk) / dz - (Ez_ijp1k - Ez_ijk) / dy\n        H[i, j, k, _HX] = m_decay_xx[i, j, k] * H[i, j, k, _HX] + m_drive_xx[i, j, k] * curl_Hx\n\n        # ---- Hy component ----\n        Ez_ijk_Hy = E[i, j, k, _EZ]\n        Ez_ip1jk = E[i + 1, j, k, _EZ]\n        Ex_ijk_Hy = E[i, j, k, _EX]\n        Ex_ijkp1 = E[i, j, k + 1, _EX]\n        curl_Hy = (Ez_ip1jk - Ez_ijk_Hy) / dx - (Ex_ijkp1 - Ex_ijk_Hy) / dz\n        H[i, j, k, _HY] = m_decay_yy[i, j, k] * H[i, j, k, _HY] + m_drive_yy[i, j, k] * curl_Hy\n\n        # ---- Hz component ----\n        Ex_ijk_Hz = E[i, j, k, _EX]\n        Ex_ijp1k = E[i, j + 1, k, _EX]\n        Ey_ijk_Hz = E[i, j, k, _EY]\n        Ey_ip1jk = E[i + 1, j, k, _EY]\n        curl_Hz = (Ex_ijp1k - Ex_ijk_Hz) / dy - (Ey_ip1jk - Ey_ijk_Hz) / dx\n        H[i, j, k, _HZ] = m_decay_zz[i, j, k] * H[i, j, k, _HZ] + m_drive_zz[i, j, k] * curl_Hz\n\nelse:\n    # Stub definitions when Warp is unavailable\n    electric_update_3d = None\n    magnetic_update_3d = None\n    electric_update_3d_coeff = None\n    magnetic_update_3d_coeff = None\n    pole_residue_electric_update_3d = None\n    pole_residue_polarization_update_3d = None\n    anisotropic_electric_update_3d = None\n    anisotropic_magnetic_update_3d = None\n\n\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n# NumPy fallback implementations (for CPU execution without Warp)\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n\ndef numpy_electric_update_3d(\n    E: np.ndarray,\n    H: np.ndarray,\n    eps_xx: np.ndarray,\n    eps_yy: np.ndarray,\n    eps_zz: np.ndarray,\n    dt: float,\n    dx: float,\n    dy: float,\n    dz: float,\n) -> None:\n    \"\"\"NumPy implementation of 3D electric field update.\n\n    Updates Ex, Ey, Ez at interior cells using the curl of H.\n    Ghost cells (index 0 and -1 on each axis) are not updated.\n\n    Array layout: E and H have shape (nx, ny, nz, 3) with\n    component order (Ex=0, Ey=1, Ez=2) for E and (Hx=0, Hy=1, Hz=2) for H.\n\n    Args:\n        E: Electric field array with shape (nx, ny, nz, 3).\n        H: Magnetic field array with shape (nx, ny, nz, 3).\n        eps_xx, eps_yy, eps_zz: Permittivity arrays with shape (nx, ny, nz).\n        dt: Timestep size.\n        dx, dy, dz: Cell sizes.\n    \"\"\"\n    nx, ny, nz = E.shape[:3]\n    # Interior indices: [1, nx-2], [1, ny-2], [1, nz-2]\n    i1, i2 = 1, nx - 1\n    j1, j2 = 1, ny - 1\n    k1, k2 = 1, nz - 1\n\n    # Precompute 1/dy, 1/dz, 1/dx for efficiency\n    inv_dy = 1.0 / dy\n    inv_dz = 1.0 / dz\n    inv_dx = 1.0 / dx\n\n    # ---- Ex component update ----\n    # Ex: dHz/dy - dHy/dz\n    # dHz_dy = (Hz[:, j+1, :, 2] - Hz[:, j, :, 2]) / dy  at i,j,k\n    # dHy_dz = (Hy[:, :, k+1, 1] - Hy[:, :, k, 1]) / dz  at i,j,k\n    Hz = H[:, :, :, 2]  # shape (nx, ny, nz)\n    Hy = H[:, :, :, 1]  # shape (nx, ny, nz)\n\n    dHz_dy_Ex = (Hz[i1:i2, j1+1:j2+1, k1:k2] - Hz[i1:i2, j1:j2, k1:k2]) * inv_dy\n    dHy_dz_Ex = (Hy[i1:i2, j1:j2, k1+1:k2+1] - Hy[i1:i2, j1:j2, k1:k2]) * inv_dz\n\n    ce_Ex = dt / (eps_xx[i1:i2, j1:j2, k1:k2] + 1e-20)\n    E[i1:i2, j1:j2, k1:k2, 0] += ce_Ex * (dHz_dy_Ex - dHy_dz_Ex)\n\n    # ---- Ey component update ----\n    # Ey: dHx/dz - dHz/dx\n    Hx = H[:, :, :, 0]  # shape (nx, ny, nz)\n\n    dHx_dz_Ey = (Hx[i1:i2, j1:j2, k1+1:k2+1] - Hx[i1:i2, j1:j2, k1:k2]) * inv_dz\n    dHz_dx_Ey = (Hz[i1+1:i2+1, j1:j2, k1:k2] - Hz[i1:i2, j1:j2, k1:k2]) * inv_dx\n\n    ce_Ey = dt / (eps_yy[i1:i2, j1:j2, k1:k2] + 1e-20)\n    E[i1:i2, j1:j2, k1:k2, 1] += ce_Ey * (dHx_dz_Ey - dHz_dx_Ey)\n\n    # ---- Ez component update ----\n    # Ez: dHy/dx - dHx/dy\n    dHy_dx_Ez = (Hy[i1+1:i2+1, j1:j2, k1:k2] - Hy[i1:i2, j1:j2, k1:k2]) * inv_dx\n    dHx_dy_Ez = (Hx[i1:i2, j1+1:j2+1, k1:k2] - Hx[i1:i2, j1:j2, k1:k2]) * inv_dy\n\n    ce_Ez = dt / (eps_zz[i1:i2, j1:j2, k1:k2] + 1e-20)\n    E[i1:i2, j1:j2, k1:k2, 2] += ce_Ez * (dHy_dx_Ez - dHx_dy_Ez)\n\n\ndef numpy_magnetic_update_3d(\n    H: np.ndarray,\n    E: np.ndarray,\n    mu_xx: np.ndarray,\n    mu_yy: np.ndarray,\n    mu_zz: np.ndarray,\n    dt: float,\n    dx: float,\n    dy: float,\n    dz: float,\n) -> None:\n    \"\"\"NumPy implementation of 3D magnetic field update.\n\n    Updates Hx, Hy, Hz at interior cells using the curl of E.\n    Ghost cells (index 0 and -1 on each axis) are not updated.\n\n    Array layout: E and H have shape (nx, ny, nz, 3) with\n    component order (Ex=0, Ey=1, Ez=2) for E and (Hx=0, Hy=1, Hz=2) for H.\n\n    Args:\n        H: Magnetic field array with shape (nx, ny, nz, 3).\n        E: Electric field array with shape (nx, ny, nz, 3).\n        mu_xx, mu_yy, mu_zz: Permeability arrays with shape (nx, ny, nz).\n        dt: Timestep size.\n        dx, dy, dz: Cell sizes.\n    \"\"\"\n    nx, ny, nz = H.shape[:3]\n    # Interior indices: [1, nx-2], [1, ny-2], [1, nz-2]\n    i1, i2 = 1, nx - 1\n    j1, j2 = 1, ny - 1\n    k1, k2 = 1, nz - 1\n\n    # Precompute 1/dy, 1/dz, 1/dx for efficiency\n    inv_dy = 1.0 / dy\n    inv_dz = 1.0 / dz\n    inv_dx = 1.0 / dx\n\n    # ---- Hx component update ----\n    # Hx: dEy/dz - dEz/dy\n    Ey = E[:, :, :, 1]  # shape (nx, ny, nz)\n    Ez = E[:, :, :, 2]\n\n    dEy_dz_Hx = (Ey[i1:i2, j1:j2, k1+1:k2+1] - Ey[i1:i2, j1:j2, k1:k2]) * inv_dz\n    dEz_dy_Hx = (Ez[i1:i2, j1+1:j2+1, k1:k2] - Ez[i1:i2, j1:j2, k1:k2]) * inv_dy\n\n    ch_Hx = -dt / (mu_xx[i1:i2, j1:j2, k1:k2] + 1e-20)\n    H[i1:i2, j1:j2, k1:k2, 0] += ch_Hx * (dEy_dz_Hx - dEz_dy_Hx)\n\n    # ---- Hy component update ----\n    # Hy: dEz/dx - dEx/dz\n    Ex = E[:, :, :, 0]\n\n    dEz_dx_Hy = (Ez[i1+1:i2+1, j1:j2, k1:k2] - Ez[i1:i2, j1:j2, k1:k2]) * inv_dx\n    dEx_dz_Hy = (Ex[i1:i2, j1:j2, k1+1:k2+1] - Ex[i1:i2, j1:j2, k1:k2]) * inv_dz\n\n    ch_Hy = -dt / (mu_yy[i1:i2, j1:j2, k1:k2] + 1e-20)\n    H[i1:i2, j1:j2, k1:k2, 1] += ch_Hy * (dEz_dx_Hy - dEx_dz_Hy)\n\n    # ---- Hz component update ----\n    # Hz: dEx/dy - dEy/dx\n    dEx_dy_Hz = (Ex[i1:i2, j1+1:j2+1, k1:k2] - Ex[i1:i2, j1:j2, k1:k2]) * inv_dy\n    dEy_dx_Hz = (Ey[i1+1:i2+1, j1:j2, k1:k2] - Ey[i1:i2, j1:j2, k1:k2]) * inv_dx\n\n    ch_Hz = -dt / (mu_zz[i1:i2, j1:j2, k1:k2] + 1e-20)\n    H[i1:i2, j1:j2, k1:k2, 2] += ch_Hz * (dEx_dy_Hz - dEy_dx_Hz)\n\n\ndef allocate_maxwell_arrays(\n    grid_shape: tuple[int, int, int],\n    *,\n    complex_policy: ComplexFieldPolicy | None = None,\n    device: int | None = None,\n    chunk_index: tuple[int, int, int] | None = None,\n) -> dict[str, \"wp.array | np.ndarray\"]:\n    \"\"\"Allocate all field and coefficient arrays for a Maxwell stepper.\n\n    Parameters\n    ----------\n    grid_shape : tuple[int, int, int]\n        Grid dimensions (Nx, Ny, Nz).\n    complex_policy : ComplexFieldPolicy, optional\n        Determines whether fields use complex dtype.\n    device : int, optional\n        CUDA device ID for allocation.\n    chunk_index : tuple, optional\n        Logical chunk position for tagging.\n\n    Returns\n    -------\n    dict[str, wp.array | np.ndarray]\n        Dictionary of allocated arrays: E, H, eps_xx, eps_yy, eps_zz,\n        mu_xx, mu_yy, mu_zz.\n    \"\"\"\n    if complex_policy is None:\n        complex_policy = ComplexFieldPolicy()\n\n    if WARP_AVAILABLE:\n        # Always return numpy arrays for allocate_maxwell_arrays.\n        # The Warp path is used only via step_maxwell() which accepts\n        # wp.array inputs directly. Tests for numpy_electric_update_3d\n        # and numpy_magnetic_update_3d expect numpy arrays.\n        import numpy as np\n\n        arrays = {}\n        arrays[\"E\"] = np.zeros((*grid_shape, 3), dtype=np.float64)\n        arrays[\"H\"] = np.zeros((*grid_shape, 3), dtype=np.float64)\n        for axis, name in [(\"xx\", \"eps_xx\"), (\"yy\", \"eps_yy\"), (\"zz\", \"eps_zz\")]:\n            arrays[name] = np.zeros(grid_shape, dtype=np.float64)\n        for axis, name in [(\"xx\", \"mu_xx\"), (\"yy\", \"mu_yy\"), (\"zz\", \"mu_zz\")]:\n            arrays[name] = np.zeros(grid_shape, This is a convenience runner for vacuum propagation tests that\n    exercises the complete E/H update cycle and collects metrics.\n\n    Parameters\n    ----------\n    arrays : dict[str, of diagnostics dicts at each ``interval`` step.\n    \"\"\"\n    results = []\n    nx, ny, nz = arrays[\"E\"].shape[:3]\n    num_cells = nx * ny * nz\n\n    for step in range(num_steps):\n          mu_xx = arrays[\"mu_xx\"]\n\n            # E energy = 0.5 * sum(eps * |E|^2) over interior cells\n            i1, i2 = 1, nx - 1\n            j1, j2 = 1, ny - 1\n            k1, k2 = 1, nz - 1\n\n            E_sq = np.sum(E[i1:i2, j1:j2, k1:k2, :] ** 2)\n            H_sq = np.sum(H[i1:i2, j1:j2, k1:k2, :] ** 2)\n\n            # Approximate vacuum energy (eps0=1, mu0=1 for normalized)\n            e_energy = 0.5 * np.mean(eps_xx[i1:i2, j1:j2, k1:k2]) * E_sq\n            h_energy = 0.5 * np.mean(mu_xx[i1:i2, j1:j2, k1:k2]) * H_sq\n\n            step_result[\"E_energy\"] = float(e_energy)\n            step_result[\"H_energy\"] = float(h_energy)\n            step_result[\"total_energy\"] = float(e_energy + h_energy)\n            results.append(step_result)\n\n    return results\n","numLines":1399,"startLine":1,"totalLines":1399}}}

{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"call_function_fascn35p8rgk_2","type":"tool_result","content":"1\t\"\"\"Scene-compilation pipeline: public API → IR → compiled runtime
