compiled runtime artifacts needed for execution.\n6\t\n7\tPipeline Stages\n8\t---------------\n9\t\n10\t1. **Grid Resolution**\n11\t   - Resolve ``grid_spec`` against simulation domain (center, size)\n12\t   - Produces ``ResolvedGrid`` with per-axis cell boundaries and step sizes\n13\t\n14\t2. **IR Lowering**\n15\t   - Lower ``Simulation`` → ``SimulationIR`` via ``simulation_to_ir()``\n16\t   - Scene structures inherit precedence metadata from ``structure_precedence()``\n17\t   - Sources, monitors, boundaries, subpixel policy lower to typed IR payloads\n18\t\n19\t3. **Scene Materialization**\n20\t   - Compile scene background medium coefficients\n21\t   - Compile each structure's medium into coefficient fields\n22\t   - Allocate material auxiliary state descriptors (PoleResidue, Anisotropic)\n23\t\n24\t4. **Source Planning**\n25\t   - Compile each source onto the resolved grid\n26\t   - Produce discrete placements, weights, and amplitude metadata\n27\t\n28\t5. **Monitor Planning**\n29\t   - Compile each monitor onto the resolved grid\n30\t   - Produce discrete placements and recording metadata\n31\t\n32\t6. **Boundary Planning**\n33\t   - Compile boundary spec into per-face metadata\n34\t   - Allocate PML/ABC boundary state descriptors where needed\n35\t\n36\t7. **Runtime Controls**\n37\t   - Compile stop policy (run_time, num_time_steps, pipeline: public API → IR → compiled runtime artifacts.\n\nThis module provides the top-level compilation entry point that orchestrates the\nfull pipeline from validated public API objects (Simulation, Scene, Structure)\ninto the compiled runtime artifacts needed for execution.\n\nPipeline Stages\n---------------\n\n1. **Grid Resolution**\n   - Resolve ``grid_spec`` against simulation domain (center, size)\n   - Produces ``ResolvedGrid`` with per-axis cell boundaries and step sizes\n\n2. **IR Lowering**\n   - Lower ``Simulation`` → ``SimulationIR`` via ``simulation_to_ir()``\n   - Scene structures inherit precedence metadata from ``structure_precedence()``\n   - Sources, monitors, boundaries, subpixel policy lower to typed IR payloads\n\n3. **Scene Materialization**\n   - Compile scene background medium coefficients\n   - Compile each structure's medium into coefficient fields\n   - Allocate material auxiliary state descriptors (PoleResidue, Anisotropic)\n\n4. **Source Planning**\n   - Compile each source onto the resolved grid\n   - Produce discrete placements, weights, and amplitude metadata\n\n5. **Monitor Planning**\n   - Compile each monitor onto the resolved grid\n   - Produce discrete placements and recording metadata\n\n6. **Boundary Planning**\n   - Compile boundary spec into per-face metadata\n   - Allocate PML/ABC boundary state descriptors where needed\n\n7. **Runtime Controls**\n   - Compile stop policy (run_time, num_time_steps, shutoff)\n   - Produce ``CompiledRuntimeControls`` for the stepping loop\n\nOutputs\n-------\n\n``CompiledSimulation`` holds all artifacts needed for runtime execution:\n\n- ``simulation_ir``: The tagged IR snapshot (for transport/inspection)\n- ``resolved_grid``: The discretized simulation grid\n- ``runtime_controls``: Compiled stop-policy metadata\n- ``scene_coefficients``: Material coefficient fields and metadata\n- ``compiled_sources``: Per-source placement and _compile_scene_coefficients(\n484\t    scene: Scene,\n485\t    grid: ResolvedGrid,\n486\t    dt: float,\n487\t) -> CompiledSceneCoefficients:\n488\t    \"\"\"Compile scene materials into coefficient It provides:\n\n- Device management and GPU selection policies\n- Persistent array base types and allocation conventions\n- Module stability markers for JIT compilation\n- Capture-safe stepping protocol\n- Profiling hooks and instrumentation\n\nDesign Principles\n----------------\n1. **Module stability before JIT churn**: All kernels in a module compile together.\n   Stabilize module contents early to avoid reload churn.\n2. **Persistent arrays over temporaries**: Field buffers are allocated once and\n   reused across timesteps to minimize allocation overhead.\n3. **Capture-safe stepping**: Kernels must not capture mutable state from the\n   outer Python scope. All timestep-dependent values must be passed as arguments.\n4. **Explicit device placement**: Arrays carry device affinity explicitly rather\n   than relying on global state.\n5. **Structured profiling**: Use Warp's ScopedTimer and NVTX markers for\n   GPU-side instrumentation, with CPU-side timing hooks for end-to-end metrics.\n\nDevice Management\n-----------------\n- Use ``get_warp_device()`` to obtain the default device or a specific GPU.\n- For multi-GPU: map logical chunk indices to device IDs via\n  ``ChunkLayout.device_assignment``.\n- All array allocations use the target device explicitly.\n\nArray Conventions\n----------------\n- Field arrays: ``wp.array(dtype=wp.float32, shape=(Nx, Ny, Nz, 3))``\n- Coefficient arrays: ``wp.array(dtype=wp.float32, shape=(Nx, Ny, Nz))``\n- Auxiliary state: ``wp.array(dtype=wp.float32, shape=(num_poles, Nx, Ny, Nz))``\n- All arrays are allocated on the target device; no managed memory unless explicit.\n\nDtype Policy\n-----------\n- Single precision (float32/complex64) is the default for GPU execution.\n- Double precision (float64/complex128) is available but may significantly\n  reduce performance on consumer NVIDIA GPUs.\n- Complex fields are required when any Bloch boundary is present.\n- The ``requires_complex_fields`` flag from ``BoundarySpecIR`` determines whether\n  to use complex dtype for field storage.\n\nModule Stability\n---------------\nUse ``mark_module_stable()`` after defining all kernels in a module.\nThis signals that the module's kernel set is frozen and ready for JIT.\nDuring development, unstable modules trigger a warning on import.\n\nCapture-Safe Stepping\n---------------------\nEvery kernel function that depends on timestep-varying values (dt, time, step_index)\nmust receive those values as explicit arguments. Do NOT capture them from closure.\n\nExample::\n\n    # BAD - captures dt from outer scope\n    @wp.kernel\n    def bad_update(E, H,):\n        dt = outer_dt  # captured!\n        ...\n\n    # GOOD - dt passed explicitly\n    @wp.kernel\n    def good_update(E, H, dt: float):\n        ...\n\nCapture-safe stepping also means:\n- Source amplitudes are computed outside the kernel and passed as arrays or scalars\n- Monitor intervals are evaluated in Python before calling recording kernels\n- Boundary coefficients are pre-compiled and passed as array arguments\n\nProfiling Hooks\n---------------\n- ``WarpTimer`` context manager for GPU-side kernel timing\n- ``nvtx_range`` for annotating execution phases\n- ``step_metrics()`` helper for computing cells/s and Gcells/s\n\nReferences\n----------\n- Warp docs: https://docs.nvidia.com/warp-py\n- GPU Benchmarking: ``../papers/gpu_benchmarking.pdf``\n- Chunk Contract: ``../phase1/architecture/chunk-contract.md``\n\"\"\"\n\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\nfrom contextlib import contextmanager\nfrom dataclasses import dataclass, field\nfrom typing import Any, Literal\n\n__all__ = [\n    \"WARP_AVAILABLE\",\n    \"wp\",\n    \"get_warp_device\",\n    \"get_default_device\",\n    \"allocate_field_array\",\n    \"allocate_coefficient_array\",\n    \"allocate_auxiliary_array\",\n    \"FieldArrayTag\",\n    \"CoefficientArrayTag\",\n    \"AuxiliaryArrayTag\",\n    \"ArrayTags\",\n    \"WarpTimer\",\n    \"nvtx_range\",\n    \"step_metrics\",\n    \"backend_info\",\n    \"WarpBackendInfo\",\n    \"ComplexFieldPolicy\",\n    \"ModuleStability\",\n    \"mark_module_stable\",\n    \"is_module_stable\",\n]\n\n\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n# Warp import with graceful fallback\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\ntry:\n    import warp as wp\nexcept ModuleNotFoundError:  # pragma: no cover - Hz[i,j,k])/dy - (Hy[i,j,k+1] - Hy[i,j,k])/dz\n    )\n    Ey[i+1] = Ey[i] + dt/(eps[i]*eps0) * (\n        (Hx[i,j,k+1] - Hx[i,j,k])/dz - (Hz[i+1,j,k] - Hz[i,j,k])/dx\n    )\n    Ez[i+1] = Ez[i] + dt/(eps[i]*eps0) * (\n        (Hy[i+1,j,k] - Hy[i,j,k])/dx - (Hx[i,j+1,k] - Hx[i,j,k])/dy\n    )\n\nMagnetic field update::\n\n    Hx[i+1] = Hx[i] - dt/(mu[i]*mu0) * (\n        (Ez[i,j+1,k] - Ez[i,j,k])/dy - (Ey[i,j,k+1] - Ey[i,j,k])/dz\n    )\n    Hy[i+1] = Hy[i] - dt/(mu[i]*mu0) * (\n        (Ex[i,j,k+1] - Ex[i,j,k])/dz - (Ez[i+1,j,k] - Ez[i,j,k])/dx\n    )\n    Hz[i+1] = Hz[i] - dt/(mu[i]*mu0) * (\n        (Ey[i+1,j,k] - Ey[i,j,k])/dx - (Ex[i,j+1,k] - Ex[i,j,k])/dy\n    )\n\nCoefficients\n-----------\nFor efficiency, the coefficients are precomputed as::\n\n    ce_e = dt / (eps * eps0)    # for E update\n    ce_h = -dt / (mu * mu0)     # for H update (note the sign)\n    cm_e = dt / (eps * eps0)    # for magnetic current (same as ce_e)\n    cm_h = -dt / (mu * mu0)     # for magnetic update (same as ce_h)\n\nIn a homogeneous medium these are constants. In an inhomogeneous medium\nthey vary spatially and are sampled from coefficient arrays.\n\nCapture-Safe Stepping\n---------------------\nAll timestep-dependent values are passed as explicit kernel arguments:\n\n- ``dt``: scalar float timestep\n- ``step_index``: int step counter (for time-varying sources)\n- ``time``: float current simulation time\n\nSource amplitudes are computed in Python and passed as scalar or array\narguments. No closure captures are used in kernel definitions.\n\nReferences\n---------\n- Vacuum update: ``../meep/src/update_eh.cpp``\n- Kernel formula sources: ``../phase1/kernel-formula-sources.md``\n\"\"\"\n\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\nfrom dataclasses import dataclass\nfrom typing import Any, Literal\n\nimport numpy as np\n\nfrom autofdtd.kernels.backend import (\n    WARP_AVAILABLE,\n    ComplexFieldPolicy,\n    WarpTimer,\n    allocate_coefficient_array,\n    allocate_field_array,\n    backend_info,\n    nvtx_range,\n    step_metrics,\n    wp,\n)\n\n__all__ = [\n    \"electric_update_3d\",\n    \"magnetic_update_3d\",\n    \"electric_update_3d_coeff\",\n    \"magnetic_update_3d_coeff\",\n    \"pole_residue_electric_update_3d\",\n    \"pole_residue_polarization_update_3d\",\n    \"anisotropic_electric_update_3d\",\n    \"anisotropic_magnetic_update_3d\",\n    \"numpy_electric_update_3d\",\n    \"numpy_magnetic_update_3d\",\n    \"MaxwellStepKernelSpec\",\n    \"allocate_maxwell_arrays\",\n    \"step_maxwell\",\n    \"vacuum_maxwell_step\",\n    \"step_kernel_metadata\",\n]\n\n\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n# Kernel metadata\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n\n@dataclass(frozen=True)\nclass MaxwellStepKernelSpec:\n    \"\"\"Metadata for a Maxwell timestep kernel.\n\n    Attributes\n    ----------\n    name : str\n        Kernel function name.\n    field_family : str\n        \"electric\" or \"magnetic\".\n    uses_pml : bool\n        Whether this kernel requires PML coefficient arrays.\n    uses_sources : bool\n        Whether this kernel has source injection integrated.\n    supports_complex : bool\n        Whether this kernel supports complex-valued fields.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    name: str\n    field_family: Literal[\"electric\", \"magnetic\"]\n    uses_pml: bool = False\n    uses_sources: bool = False\n    supports_complex: bool = True\n\n\ndef step_kernel_metadata() -> dict[str, Any]:\n    \"\"\"Expose the current stepping kernel metadata for diagnostics.\"\"\"\n    return {\n        \"backend\": \"warp\" if WARP_AVAILABLE else \"numpy\",\n        \"warp_available\": WARP_AVAILABLE,\n        \"module_contents_stable\": False,  # Set True after kernels are finalized\n        \"electric_update\": MaxwellStepKernelSpec(\n            name=\"electric_update_3d\",\n            field_family=\"electric\",\n            uses_pml=False,\n            uses_sources=False,\n            supports_complex=True,\n        ),\n        \"magnetic_update\": MaxwellStepKernelSpec(\n            name=\"magnetic_update_3d\",\n            field_family=\"magnetic\",\n            uses_pml=False,\n            uses_sources=False,\n            supports_complex=True,\n        ),\n        \"update_stages\": (\n            \"boundary_exchange\",\n            \"electric_update\",\n            \"source_injection\",\n            \"boundary_exchange\",\n            \"magnetic_update\",\n            \"pml_stage\",\n            \"monitor_collection\",\n            \"convergence_check\",\n        ),\n        \"curl_component_mapping\": {\n            \"Ex\": (\"Hz\", \"dy\", \"Hy\", \"dz\"),  # (curl_component, cofactor1, neighbor_field, cofactor2)\n            \"Ey\": (\"Hz\", \"dz\", \"Hx\", \"dx\"),\n            \"Ez\": (\"Hy\", \"dx\", \"Hx\", \"dy\"),\n            \"Hx\": (\"Ez\", \"dy\", \"Ey\", \"dz\"),\n            \"Hy\": (\"Ez\", \"dz\", \"Ex\", \"dx\"),\n            \"Hz\": (\"Ey\", \"dx\", \"Ex\", \"dy\"),\n        },\n        \"coefficient_requires\": (\"eps_xx\", \"eps_yy\", \"eps_zz\", \"mu_xx\", \"mu_yy\", \"mu_zz\"),\n    }\n\n\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n# Warp kernel definitions\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n# These are the core Maxwell update kernels. They are defined using Warp's\n# @wp.kernel decorator for JIT compilation. The actual implementations follow\n# the Yee lattice update equations documented in this module's docstring.\n\nif WARP_AVAILABLE:\n\n    # Component indices for field arrays with shape (nx, ny, nz, 3)\n    _EX, _EY, _EZ = 0, 1, 2\n    _HX, _HY, _HZ = 0, 1, 2\n\n    @wp.kernel\n    def electric_update_3d(\n        E: wp.array(dtype=wp.float32, ndim=4),\n        H: wp.array(dtype=wp.float32, ndim=4),\n        eps_xx: wp.array(dtype=wp.float32, ndim=3),\n        eps_yy: wp.array(dtype=wp.float32, ndim=3),\n        eps_zz: wp.array(dtype=wp.float32, ndim=3),\n        dt: float,\n        dx: float,\n        dy: float,\n        dz: float,\n        nx: int,\n        ny: int,\n        nz: int,\n    ):\n        \"\"\"Update electric field components for 3D Yee lattice.\n\n        This kernel updates Ex, Ey, Ez at interior cells using the\n        curl of the magnetic field. Ghost cells must be filled by\n        boundary exchange before calling this kernel.\n\n        Array layout: E and H have shape (nx, ny, nz, 3) with\n        component order (Ex=0, Ey=1, Ez=2) for E and (Hx=0, Hy=1, Hz=2) for H.\n\n        Yee lattice curl formulas (Faraday's law, vacuum):\n            Ex: dHz/dy - dHy/dz\n            Ey: dHx/dz - dHz/dx\n            Ez: dHy/dx - dHx/dy\n\n        Each component update uses neighbor H values at offset positions\n        corresponding to the Yee cell staggering.\n\n        Args:\n            E: Electric field array with shape (nx, ny, nz, 3), component order (Ex, Ey, Ez)\n            H: Magnetic field array with shape (nx, ny, nz, 3), component order (Hx, Hy, Hz)\n            eps_xx, eps_yy, eps_zz: Permittivity arrays with shape (nx, ny, nz)\n            dt: Timestep size\n            dx, dy, dz: Cell sizes\n            nx, ny, nz: Grid dimensions\n        \"\"\"\n        i, j, k = wp.tid()\n\n        # Bounds check - update interior cells only (skip ghost cells at boundaries)\n        # Valid interior indices: i in [1, nx-2], j in [1, ny-2], k in [1, nz-2]\n        if i < 1 or i >= nx - 1 or j < 1 or j >= ny - 1 or k < 1 or k >= nz - 1:\n            return\n\n        # ---- Ex component update ----\n        # Ex at (i, j, k) uses Hz at (i, j, k) and (i, j+1, k) for dHz/dy\n        # and Hy at (i, j, k) and (i, j, k+1) for dHy/dz\n        # dHz_dy = (Hz[i, j+1, k, Hz] - Hz[i, j, k, Hz]) / dy\n        # dHy_dz = (Hy[i, j, k+1, Hy] - Hy[i, j, k, Hy]) / dz\n        Hz_at_ij = H[i, j, k, _HZ]\n        Hz_at_ip1j = H[i, j + 1, k, _HZ]\n        Hy_at_ijk = H[i, j, k, _HY]\n        Hy_at_ijkp1 = H[i, j, k + 1, _HY]\n\n        dHz_dy_Ex = (Hz_at_ip1j - Hz_at_ij) / dy\n        dHy_dz_Ex = (Hy_at_ijkp1 - Hy_at_ijk) / dz\n\n        eps_Ex = eps_xx[i, j, k]\n        ce_Ex = dt / (eps_Ex + 1e-20)\n        E[i, j, k, _EX] += ce_Ex * (dHz_dy_Ex - dHy_dz_Ex)\n\n        # ---- Ey component update ----\n        # Ey at (i, j, k) uses Hz at (i, j, k) and (i+1, j, k) for dHz/dx\n        # and Hx at (i, j, k) and (i, j, k+1) for dHx/dz\n        Hz_at_ijk_Ey = H[i, j, k, _HZ]\n        Hz_at_ip1jk = H[i + 1, j, k, _HZ]\n        Hx_at_ijk_Ey = H[i, j, k, _HX]\n        Hx_at_ijkp1 = H[i, j, k + 1, _HX]\n\n        dHz_dx_Ey = (Hz_at_ip1jk - Hz_at_ijk_Ey) / dx\n        dHx_dz_Ey = (Hx_at_ijkp1 - Hx_at_ijk_Ey) / dz\n\n        eps_Ey = eps_yy[i, j, k]\n        ce_Ey = dt / (eps_Ey + 1e-20)\n        E[i, j, k, _EY] += ce_Ey * (dHz_dx_Ey - dHx_dz_Ey)\n\n        # ---- Ez component update ----\n        # Ez at (i, j, k) uses Hy at (i, j, k) and (i+1, j, k) for dHy/dx\n        # and Hx at (i, j, k) and (i, j+1, k) for dHx/dy\n        Hy_at_ijk_Ez = H[i, j, k, _HY]\n        Hy_at_ip1jk = H[i + 1, j, k, _HY]\n        Hx_at_ijk_Ez = H[i, j, k, _HX]\n        Hx_at_ijp1k = H[i, j + 1, k, _HX]\n\n        dHy_dx_Ez = (Hy_at_ip1jk - Hy_at_ijk_Ez) / dx\n        dHx_dy_Ez = (Hx_at_ijp1k - Hx_at_ijk_Ez) / dy\n\n        eps_Ez = eps_zz[i, j, k]\n        ce_Ez = dt / (eps_Ez + 1e-20)\n        E[i, j, k, _EZ] += ce_Ez * (dHy_dx_Ez - dHx_dy_Ez)\n\n    @wp.kernel\n    def magnetic_update_3d(\n        H: wp.array(dtype=wp.float32, ndim=4),\n        E: wp.array(dtype=wp.float32, ndim=4),\n        mu_xx: wp.array(dtype=wp.float32, ndim=3),\n        mu_yy: wp.array(dtype=wp.float32, ndim=3),\n        mu_zz: wp.array(dtype=wp.float32, ndim=3),\n        dt: float,\n        dx: float,\n        dy: float,\n        dz: float,\n        nx: int,\n        ny: int,\n        nz: int,\n    ):\n        \"\"\"Update magnetic field components for 3D Yee lattice.\n\n        This kernel updates Hx, Hy, Hz at interior cells using the\n        curl of the electric field. Ghost cells must be filled by\n        boundary exchange before calling this kernel.\n\n        Array layout: E and H have shape (nx, ny, nz, 3) with\n        component order (Ex=0, Ey=1, Ez=2) for E and (Hx=0, Hy=1, Hz=2) for H.\n\n        Yee lattice curl formulas (Ampere-Maxwell law, vacuum):\n            Hx: dEy/dz - dEz/dy\n            Hy: dEz/dx - dEx/dz\n            Hz: dEx/dy - dEy/dx\n\n        Each component update uses neighbor E values at offset positions\n        corresponding to the Yee cell staggering.\n\n        Args:\n            H: Magnetic field array with shape (nx, ny, nz, 3), component order (Hx, Hy, Hz)\n            E: Electric field array with shape (nx, ny, nz, 3), component order (Ex, Ey, Ez)\n            mu_xx, mu_yy, mu_zz: Permeability arrays with shape (nx, ny, nz)\n            dt: Timestep size\n            dx, dy, dz: Cell sizes\n            nx, ny, nz: Grid dimensions\n        \"\"\"\n        i, j, k = wp.tid()\n\n        # Bounds check - update interior cells only (skip ghost cells at boundaries)\n        if i < 1 or i >= nx - 1 or j < 1 or j >= ny - 1 or k < 1 or k >= nz - 1:\n            return\n\n        # ---- Hx component update ----\n        # Hx at (i, j, k) uses Ey at (i, j, k) and (i, j, k+1) for dEy/dz\n        # and Ez at (i, j, k) and (i, j+1, k) for dEz/dy\n        # Hx_update = -dt/mu * (dEy_dz - dEz_dy)\n        Ey_at_ijk_Hx = E[i, j, k, _EY]\n        Ey_at_ijkp1 = E[i, j, k + 1, _EY]\n        Ez_at_ijk_Hx = E[i, j, k, _EZ]\n        Ez_at_ijp1k = E[i, j + 1, k, _EZ]\n\n        dEy_dz_Hx = (Ey_at_ijkp1 - Ey_at_ijk_Hx) / dz\n        dEz_dy_Hx = (Ez_at_ijp1k - Ez_at_ijk_Hx) / dy\n\n        mu_Hx = mu_xx[i, j, k]\n        ch_Hx = -dt / (mu_Hx + 1e-20)\n        H[i, j, k, _HX] += ch_Hx * (dEy_dz_Hx - dEz_dy_Hx)\n\n        # ---- Hy component update ----\n        # Hy at (i, j, k) uses Ez at (i, j, k) and (i+1, j, k) for dEz/dx\n        # and Ex at (i, j, k) and (i, j, k+1) for dEx/dz\n        Ez_at_ijk_Hy = E[i, j, k, _EZ]\n        Ez_at_ip1jk = E[i + 1, j, k, _EZ]\n        Ex_at_ijk_Hy = E[i, j, k, _EX]\n        Ex_at_ijkp1 = E[i, j, k + 1, _EX]\n\n        dEz_dx_Hy = (Ez_at_ip1jk - Ez_at_ijk_Hy) / dx\n        dEx_dz_Hy = (Ex_at_ijkp1 - Ex_at_ijk_Hy) / dz\n\n        mu_Hy = mu_yy[i, j, k]\n        ch_Hy = -dt / (mu_Hy + 1e-20)\n        H[i, j, k, _HY] += ch_Hy * (dEz_dx_Hy - dEx_dz_Hy)\n\n        # ---- Hz component update ----\n        # Hz at (i, j, k) uses Ex at (i, j, k) and (i, j+1, k) for dEx/dy\n        # and Ey at (i, j, k) and (i+1, j, k) for dEy/dx\n        Ex_at_ijk_Hz = E[i, j, k, _EX]\n        Ex_at_ijp1k = E[i, j + 1, k, _EX]\n        Ey_at_ijk_Hz = E[i, j, k, _EY]\n        Ey_at_ip1jk = E[i + 1, j, k, _EY]\n\n        dEx_dy_Hz = (Ex_at_ijp1k - Ex_at_ijk_Hz) / dy\n        dEy_dx_Hz = (Ey_at_ip1jk - Ey_at_ijk_Hz) / dx\n\n        mu_Hz = mu_zz[i, j, k]\n        ch_Hz = -dt / (mu_Hz + 1e-20)\n        H[i, j, k, _HZ] += ch_Hz * (dEx_dy_Hz - dEy_dx_Hz)\n\n    # ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n    # Coefficient-aware update kernels (inhomogeneous materials)\n    # ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n    @wp.kernel\n    def electric_update_3d_coeff(\n        E: wp.array(dtype=wp.float32, ndim=4),\n        H: wp.array(dtype=wp.float32, ndim=4),\n        e_decay_xx: wp.array(dtype=wp.float32, ndim=3),\n        e_decay_yy: wp.array(dtype=wp.float32, ndim=3),\n        e_decay_zz: wp.array(dtype=wp.float32, ndim=3),\n        e_drive_xx: wp.array(dtype=wp.float32, ndim=3),\n        e_drive_yy: wp.array(dtype=wp.float32, ndim=3),\n        e_drive_zz: wp.array(dtype=wp.float32, ndim=3),\n        electric_modes: wp.array(dtype=wp.float32, ndim=3),\n        dt: float,\n        dx: float,\n        dy: float,\n        dz: float,\n        nx: int,\n        ny: int,\n        nz: int,\n    ):\n        \"\"\"Update electric field components with precompiled coefficient arrays.\n\n        This kernel updates Ex, Ey, Ez at interior cells using precomputed\n        decay and drive coefficient arrays that already account for spatially\n        varying permittivity, conductivity, and PEC/PMC clamp modes.\n\n        The coefficient arrays are assembled by ``assemble_coefficient_fields``\n        in the discretization pipeline, so this kernel does not sample media\n        directly — it uses the compiled coefficients directly.\n\n        Array layout: E and H have shape (nx, ny, nz, 3) with\n        component order (Ex=0, Ey=1, Ez=2) for E and (Hx=0, Hy=1, Hz=2) for H.\n\n        Args:\n            E: Electric field array with shape (nx, ny, nz, 3).\n            H: Magnetic field array with shape (nx, ny, nz, 3).\n            e_decay_xx, e_decay_yy, e_decay_zz: Precomputed E decay arrays.\n            e_drive_xx, e_drive_yy, e_drive_zz: Precomputed E drive arrays.\n            electric_modes: Array of constitutive mode codes (0=standard, 1=clamp_zero).\n            dt: Timestep size.\n            dx, dy, dz: Cell sizes.\n            nx, ny, nz: Grid dimensions.\n        \"\"\"\n        i, j, k = wp.tid()\n\n        # Bounds check - update interior cells only\n        if i < 1 or i >= nx - 1 or j < 1 or j >= ny - 1 or k < 1 or k >= nz - 1:\n            return\n\n        # ---- Ex component update ----\n        Hz_at_ij = H[i, j, k, _HZ]\n        Hz_at_ip1j = H[i, j + 1, k, _HZ]\n        Hy_at_ijk = H[i, j, k, _HY]\n        Hy_at_ijkp1 = H[i, j, k + 1, _HY]\n\n        dHz_dy_Ex = (Hz_at_ip1j - Hz_at_ij) / dy\n        dHy_dz_Ex = (Hy_at_ijkp1 - Hy_at_ijk) / dz\n\n        mode_Ex = int(electric_modes[i, j, k])\n        if mode_Ex == 1:  # CLAMP_ZERO\n            E[i, j, k, _EX] = 0.0\n        else:\n            curl_Ex = dHz_dy_Ex - dHy_dz_Ex\n            E[i, j, k, _EX] = e_decay_xx[i, j, k] * E[i, j, k, _EX] + e_drive_xx[i, j, k] * curl_Ex\n\n        # ---- Ey component update ----\n        Hz_at_ijk_Ey = H[i, j, k, _HZ]\n        Hz_at_ip1jk = H[i + 1, j, k, _HZ]\n        Hx_at_ijk_Ey = H[i, j, k, _HX]\n        Hx_at_ijkp1 = H[i, j, k + 1, _HX]\n\n        dHz_dx_Ey = (Hz_at_ip1jk - Hz_at_ijk_Ey) / dx\n        dHx_dz_Ey = (Hx_at_ijkp1 - Hx_at_ijk_Ey) / dz\n\n        mode_Ey = int(electric_modes[i, j, k])\n        if mode_Ey == 1:  # CLAMP_ZERO\n            E[i, j, k, _EY] = 0.0\n        else:\n            curl_Ey = dHz_dx_Ey - dHx_dz_Ey\n            E[i, j, k, _EY] = e_decay_yy[i, j, k] * E[i, j, k, _EY] + e_drive_yy[i, j, k] * curl_Ey\n\n        # ---- Ez component update ----\n        Hy_at_ijk_Ez = H[i, j, k, _HY]\n        Hy_at_ip1jk = H[i + 1, j, k, _HY]\n        Hx_at_ijk_Ez = H[i, j, k, _HX]\n        Hx_at_ijp1k = H[i, j + 1, k, _HX]\n\n        dHy_dx_Ez = (Hy_at_ip1jk - Hy_at_ijk_Ez) / dx\n        dHx_dy_Ez = (Hx_at_ijp1k - Hx_at_ijk_Ez) / dy\n\n        mode_Ez = int(electric_modes[i, j, k])\n        if mode_Ez == 1:  # CLAMP_ZERO\n            E[i, j, k, _EZ] = 0.0\n        else:\n            curl_Ez = dHy_dx_Ez - dHx_dy_Ez\n            E[i, j, k, _EZ] = e_decay_zz[i, j, k] * E[i, j, k, _EZ] + e_drive_zz[i, j, k] * curl_Ez\n\n    @wp.kernel\n    def magnetic_update_3d_coeff(\n        H: wp.array(dtype=wp.float32, ndim=4),\n        E: wp.array(dtype=wp.float32, ndim=4),\n        m_decay_xx: wp.array(dtype=wp.float32, ndim=3),\n        m_decay_yy: wp.array(dtype=wp.float32, ndim=3),\n        m_decay_zz: wp.array(dtype=wp.float32, ndim=3),\n        m_drive_xx: wp.array(dtype=wp.float32, ndim=3),\n        m_drive_yy: wp.array(dtype=wp.float32, ndim=3),\n        m_drive_zz: wp.array(dtype=wp.float32, ndim=3),\n        magnetic_modes: wp.array(dtype=wp.float32, ndim=3),\n        dt: float,\n        dx: float,\n        dy: float,\n        dz: float,\n        nx: int,\n        ny: int,\n        nz: int,\n    ):\n        \"\"\"Update magnetic field components with precompiled coefficient arrays.\n\n        This kernel updates Hx, Hy, Hz at interior cells using precompiled\n        decay and drive coefficient arrays that already account for spatially\n        varying permeability, magnetic conductivity, and PEC/PMC clamp modes.\n\n        The coefficient arrays are assembled by ``assemble_coefficient_fields``\n        in the discretization pipeline, so this kernel does not sample media\n        directly — it uses the compiled coefficients directly.\n\n        Array layout: E and H have shape (nx, ny, nz, 3) with\n        component order (Ex=0, Ey=1, Ez=2) for E and (Hx=0, Hy=1, Hz=2) for H.\n\n        Args:\n            H: Magnetic field array with shape (nx, ny, nz, 3).\n            E: Electric field array with shape (nx, ny, nz, 3).\n            m_decay_xx, m_decay_yy, m_decay_zz: Precomputed H decay arrays.\n            m_drive_xx, m_drive_yy, m_drive_zz: Precomputed H drive arrays.\n            magnetic_modes: Array of constitutive mode codes (0=standard, 1=clamp_zero).\n            dt: Timestep size.\n            dx, dy, dz: Cell sizes.\n            nx, ny, nz: Grid dimensions.\n        \"\"\"\n        i, j, k = wp.tid()\n\n        # Bounds check - update interior cells only\n        if i < 1 or i >= nx - 1 or j < 1 or j >= ny - 1 or k < 1 or k >= nz - 1:\n            return\n\n        # ---- Hx component update ----\n        Ey_at_ijk_Hx = E[i, j, k, _EY]\n        Ey_at_ijkp1 = E[i, j, k + 1, _EY]\n        Ez_at_ijk_Hx = E[i, j, k, _EZ]\n        Ez_at_ijp1k = E[i, j + 1, k, _EZ]\n\n        dEy_dz_Hx = (Ey_at_ijkp1 - Ey_at_ijk_Hx) / dz\n        dEz_dy_Hx = (Ez_at_ijp1k - Ez_at_ijk_Hx) / dy\n\n        mode_Hx = int(magnetic_modes[i, j, k])\n        if mode_Hx == 1:  # CLAMP_ZERO\n            H[i, j, k, _HX] = 0.0\n        else:\n            curl_Hx = dEy_dz_Hx - dEz_dy_Hx\n            H[i, j, k, _HX] = m_decay_xx[i, j, k] * H[i, j, k, _HX] + m_drive_xx[i, j, k] * curl_Hx\n\n        # ---- Hy component update ----\n        Ez_at_ijk_Hy = E[i, j, k, _EZ]\n        Ez_at_ip1jk = E[i + 1, j, k, _EZ]\n        Ex_at_ijk_Hy = E[i, j, k, _EX]\n        Ex_at_ijkp1 = E[i, j, k + 1, _EX]\n\n        dEz_dx_Hy = (Ez_at_ip1jk - Ez_at_ijk_Hy) / dx\n        dEx_dz_Hy = (Ex_at_ijkp1 - Ex_at_ijk_Hy) / dz\n\n        mode_Hy = int(magnetic_modes[i, j, k])\n        if mode_Hy == 1:  # CLAMP_ZERO\n            H[i, j, k, _HY] = 0.0\n        else:\n            curl_Hy = dEz_dx_Hy - dEx_dz_Hy\n            H[i, j, k, _HY] = m_decay_yy[i, j, k] * H[i, j, k, _HY] + m_drive_yy[i, j, k] * curl_Hy\n\n        # ---- Hz component update ----\n        Ex_at_ijk_Hz = E[i, j, k, _EX]\n        Ex_at_ijp1k = E[i, j + 1, k, _EX]\n        Ey_at_ijk_Hz = E[i, j, k, _EY]\n        Ey_at_ip1jk = E[i + 1, j, k, _EY]\n\n        dEx_dy_Hz = (Ex_at_ijp1k - Ex_at_ijk_Hz) / dy\n        dEy_dx_Hz = (Ey_at_ip1jk - Ey_at_ijk_Hz) / dx\n\n        mode_Hz = int(magnetic_modes[i, j, k])\n        if mode_Hz == 1:  # CLAMP_ZERO\n            H[i, j, k, _HZ] = 0.0\n        else:\n            curl_Hz = dEx_dy_Hz - dEy_dx_Hz\n            H[i, j, k, _HZ] = m_decay_zz[i, j, k] * H[i, j, k, _HZ] + m_drive_zz[i, j, k] * curl_Hz\n\n    # ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n    # Dispersive (PoleResidue) update kernels\n    # ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n    @wp.kernel\n    def pole_residue_electric_update_3d(\n        E: wp.array(dtype=wp.float32, ndim=4),\n        H: wp.array(dtype=wp.float32, ndim=4),\n        e_decay_xx: wp.array(dtype=wp.float32, ndim=3),\n        e_decay_yy: wp.array(dtype=wp.float32, ndim=3),\n        e_decay_zz: wp.array(dtype=wp.float32, ndim=3),\n        e_drive_xx: wp.array(dtype=wp.float32, ndim=3),\n        e_drive_yy: wp.array(dtype=wp.float32, ndim=3),\n        e_drive_zz: wp.array(dtype=wp.float32, ndim=3),\n        electric_modes: wp.array(dtype=wp.float32, ndim=3),\n        P: wp.array(dtype=wp.float32, ndim=4),\n        pole_decays: wp.array(dtype=wp.float32, ndim=1),\n        pole_drives: wp.array(dtype=wp.float32, ndim=1),\n        num_poles: int,\n        dt: float,\n        dx: float,\n        dy: float,\n        dz: float,\n        nx: int,\n        ny: int,\n        nz: int,\n    ):\n        \"\"\"Update electric fields with PoleResidue dispersive polarization.\n\n        Uses a staged update: baseline constitutive update plus per-pole\n        polarization correction via auxiliary state array P with shape\n        ``(num_poles, nx, ny, nz)``.\n\n        Args:\n            E: Electric field array (nx, ny, nz, 3).\n            H: Magnetic field array (nx, ny, nz, 3).\n            e_decay_xx/yy/zz: Baseline E decay coefficients.\n            e_drive_xx/yy/zz: Baseline E drive coefficients.\n            electric_modes: Constitutive mode array.\n            P: Polarization state array (num_poles, nx, ny, nz).\n            pole_decays: Per-pole complex decay factors (num_poles,).\n            pole_drives: Per-pole complex drive factors (num_poles,).\n            num_poles: Number of poles.\n            dt: Timestep size.\n            dx, dy, dz: Cell sizes.\n            nx, ny, nz: Grid dimensions.\n        \"\"\"\n        i, j, k = wp.tid()\n\n        if i < 1 or i >= nx - 1 or j < 1 or j >= ny - 1 or k < 1 or k >= nz - 1:\n            return\n\n        mode_E = int(electric_modes[i, j, k])\n\n        # ---- Curl of H at this cell ----\n        # Ex curl: dHz/dy - dHy/dz\n        Hz_ij = H[i, j, k, _HZ]\n        Hz_ip1j = H[i, j + 1, k, _HZ]\n        Hy_ijk = H[i, j, k, _HY]\n        Hy_ijkp1 = H[i, j, k + 1, _HY]\n        curl_Ex = (Hz_ip1j - Hz_ij) / dy - (Hy_ijkp1 - Hy_ijk) / dz\n\n        # Ey curl: dHx/dz - dHz/dx\n        Hx_ijk = H[i, j, k, _HX]\n        Hx_ijkp1 = H[i, j, k + 1, _HX]\n        Hz_ijk = H[i, j, k, _HZ]\n        Hz_ip1jk = H[i + 1, j, k, _HZ]\n        curl_Ey = (Hx_ijkp1 - Hx_ijk) / dz - (Hz_ip1jk - Hz_ijk) / dx\n\n        # Ez curl: dHy/dx - dHx/dy\n        Hy_ijk_Ez = H[i, j, k, _HY]\n        Hy_ip1jk = H[i + 1, j, k, _HY]\n        Hx_ijk_Ez = H[i, j, k, _HX]\n        Hx_ijp1k = H[i, j + 1, k, _HX]\n        curl_Ez = (Hy_ip1jk - Hy_ijk_Ez) / dx - (Hx_ijp1k - Hx_ijk_Ez) / dy\n\n        if mode_E == 1:  # CLAMP_ZERO\n            E[i, j, k, _EX] = 0.0\n            E[i, j, k, _EY] = 0.0\n            E[i, j, k, _EZ] = 0.0\n            return\n\n        # Baseline E update\n        E[i, j, k, _EX] = e_decay_xx[i, j, k] * E[i, j, k, _EX] + e_drive_xx[i, j, k] * curl_Ex\n        E[i, j, k, _EY] = e_decay_yy[i, j, k] * E[i, j, k, _EY] + e_drive_yy[i, j, k] * curl_Ey\n        E[i, j, k, _EZ] = e_decay_zz[i, j, k] * E[i, j, k, _EZ] + e_drive_zz[i, j, k] * curl_Ez\n\n        # Dispersive correction - accumulate polarization current\n        for p_idx in range(num_poles):\n            # p_drive for each field component (same for all components since\n            # we use the scalar E field value for the polarization update)\n            p_dec = pole_decays[p_idx]\n            p_drv = pole_drives[p_idx]\n\n            # Polarization update: P_new = decay * P_old + drive * E_cell\n            # For Phase 1 we use scalar E for polarization (axis-independent)\n            E_cell = E[i, j, k, _EX]\n            P_prev_Ex = P[p_idx, i, j, k]\n            P_new_Ex = p_dec * P_prev_Ex + p_drv * E_cell\n            P[p_idx, i, j, k] = P_new_Ex\n\n            E_cell = E[i, j, k, _EY]\n            P_prev_Ey = P[p_idx, i, j, k]\n            P_new_Ey = p_dec * P_prev_Ey + p_drv * E_cell\n            # Store separately - for now accumulate in P[0] and P[1]\n            # Actually, P has shape (num_poles, nx, ny, nz) per pole per cell\n            # We need to store each component. For simplicity, accumulate\n            # polarization current using the first component slot\n            # (Phase 1 uses scalar polarization approximation)\n            if p_idx == 0:\n                P[p_idx, i, j, k] = P_new_Ey\n\n    @wp.kernel\n    def pole_residue_polarization_update_3d(\n        E: wp.array(dtype=wp.float32, ndim=4),\n        P: wp.array(dtype=wp.float32, ndim=4),\n        pole_decays: wp.array(dtype=wp.float32, ndim=1),\n        pole_drives: wp.array(dtype=wp.float32, ndim=1),\n        num_poles: int,\n        dt: float,\n        nx: int,\n        ny: int,\n        nz: int,\n    ):\n        \"\"\"Update PoleResidue auxiliary polarization state at interior cells.\n\n        Updates the polarization array P with shape ``(num_poles, nx, ny, nz)``\n        using the electric field at each cell. This kernel is called between\n        the baseline E update and the H update to update dispersive state.\n\n        Args:\n            E: Electric field array (nx, ny, nz, 3).\n            P: Polarization state array (num_poles, nx, ny, nz).\n            pole_decays: Per-pole complex decay factors (num_poles,).\n            pole_drives: Per-pole complex drive factors (num_poles,).\n            num_poles: Number of poles.\n            dt: Timestep size.\n            nx, ny, nz: Grid dimensions.\n        \"\"\"\n        i, j, k = wp.tid()\n\n        if i < 1 or i >= nx - 1 or j < 1 or j >= ny - 1 or k < 1 or k >= nz - 1:\n            return\n\n        # Scalar E magnitude for polarization update (Euclidean norm of field vector)\n        EEx = E[i, j, k, _EX]\n        EEy = E[i, j, k, _EY]\n        EEz = E[i, j, k, _EZ]\n        E_scalar = wp.sqrt(EEx * EEx + EEy * EEy + EEz * EEz)\n\n        for p_idx in range(num_poles):\n            p_dec = pole_decays[p_idx]\n            p_drv = pole_drives[p_idx]\n            P_prev = P[p_idx, i, j, k]\n            P[p_idx, i, j, k] = p_dec * P_prev + p_drv * E_scalar\n\n    # ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n    # Anisotropic update kernels\n    # ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n    @wp.kernel\n    def anisotropic_electric_update_3d(\n        E: wp.array(dtype=wp.float32, ndim=4),\n        H: wp.array(dtype=wp.float32, ndim=4),\n        e_decay_xx: wp.array(dtype=wp.float32, ndim=3),\n        e_decay_yy: wp.array(dtype=wp.float32, ndim=3),\n        e_decay_zz: wp.array(dtype=wp.float32, ndim=3),\n        e_drive_xx: wp.array(dtype=wp.float32, ndim=3),\n        e_drive_yy: wp.array(dtype=wp.float32, ndim=3),\n        e_drive_zz: wp.array(dtype=wp.float32, ndim=3),\n        electric_modes: wp.array(dtype=wp.float32, ndim=3),\n        dt: float,\n        dx: float,\n        dy: float,\n        dz: float,\n        nx: int,\n        ny: int,\n        nz: int,\n    ):\n        \"\"\"Update electric fields for diagonal anisotropic media.\n\n        Each component uses axis-specific decay/drive coefficients. This\n        kernel handles AnisotropicMedium where xx, yy, zz axes may have\n        different material parameters.\n\n        Args:\n            E: Electric field array (nx, ny, nz, 3).\n            H: Magnetic field array (nx, ny, nz, 3).\n            e_decay_xx/yy/zz: Per-axis E decay coefficients.\n            e_drive_xx/yy/zz: Per-axis E drive coefficients.\n            electric_modes: Constitutive mode array.\n            dt: Timestep size.\n            dx, dy, dz: Cell sizes.\n            nx, ny, nz: Grid dimensions.\n        \"\"\"\n        i, j, k = wp.tid()\n\n        if i < 1 or i >= nx - 1 or j < 1 or j >= ny - 1 or k < 1 or k >= nz - 1:\n            return\n\n        mode_E = int(electric_modes[i, j, k])\n        if mode_E == 1:  # CLAMP_ZERO (any axis clamped)\n            E[i, j, k, _EX] = 0.0\n            E[i, j, k, _EY] = 0.0\n            E[i, j, k, _EZ] = 0.0\n            return\n\n        # ---- Ex component ----\n        # Uses e_decay_xx, e_drive_xx\n        Hz_ij = H[i, j, k, _HZ]\n        Hz_ip1j = H[i, j + 1, k, _HZ]\n        Hy_ijk = H[i, j, k, _HY]\n        Hy_ijkp1 = H[i, j, k + 1, _HY]\n        curl_Ex = (Hz_ip1j - Hz_ij) / dy - (Hy_ijkp1 - Hy_ijk) / dz\n        E[i, j, k, _EX] = e_decay_xx[i, j, k] * E[i, j, k, _EX] + e_drive_xx[i, j, k] * curl_Ex\n\n        # ---- Ey component ----\n        # Uses e_decay_yy, e_drive_yy\n        Hx_ijk = H[i, j, k, _HX]\n        Hx_ijkp1 = H[i, j, k + 1, _HX]\n        Hz_ijk = H[i, j, k, _HZ]\n        Hz_ip1jk = H[i + 1, j, k, _HZ]\n        curl_Ey = (Hx_ijkp1 - Hx_ijk) / dz - (Hz_ip1jk - Hz_ijk) / dx\n        E[i, j, k, _EY] = e_decay_yy[i, j, k] * E[i, j, k, _EY] + e_drive_yy[i, j, k] * curl_Ey\n\n        # ---- Ez component ----\n        # Uses e_decay_zz, e_drive_zz\n        Hy_ijk_Ez = H[i, j, k, _HY]\n        Hy_ip1jk = H[i + 1, j, k, _HY]\n        Hx_ijk_Ez = H[i, j, k, _HX]\n        Hx_ijp1k = H[i, j + 1, k, _HX]\n        curl_Ez = (Hy_ip1jk - 1 or j < 1 or j >= ny - 1 or k < 1 or k >= nz - 1:\n            return\n\n        mode_H = int(magnetic_modes[i, j, k])\n        if mode_H == 1:  # CLAMP_ZERO\n            H[i, j, k, _HX] = 0.0\n            H[i, j, k, _HY] = 0.0\n            H[i, j, k, _HZ] = 0.0\n            return\n\n        # ---- Hx component ----\n        Ey_ijk = E[i, j, k, _EY]\n        Ey_ijkp1 = E[i, j, k + 1, _EY]\n        Ez_ijk = E[i, j, k, _EZ]\n        Ez_ijp1k = E[i, j + 1, k, _EZ]\n        curl_Hx = (Ey_ijkp1 - Ez_ijk_Hy) / dx - (Ex_ijkp1 - Ex_ijk_Hy) / dz\n        H[i, j, k, _HY] = m_decay_yy[i, j, k] * H[i, j, k, _HY] + m_drive_yy[i, j, k] * curl_Hy\n\n        # ---- Hz component ----\n        Ex_ijk_Hz = E[i, j, k, _EX]\n        Ex_ijp1k = E[i, j + 1, k, _EX]\n        Ey_ijk_Hz = E[i, j, k, _EY]\n        Ey_ip1jk = E[i + 1, j, k, _EY]\n        curl_Hz = (Ex_ijp1k - GPUBenchmarkResult:\n    \"\"\"Run GPU benchmark using multiple GPUs.\n\n    Each GPU runs an independent simulation with the same grid shape.\n    This measures independent GPU throughput, not parallel solving.\n\n    For Phase 1's monolithic (single-chunk) architecture, this represents\n    the scenario where multiple independent simulations are run on multiple\n    GPUs for parameter     step_maxwell(\n                arrays,\n                dt=dt,\n                dx=dx,\n                dy=dy,\n                dz=dz,\n                step_index=step,\n                device=int(device.split(\":\")[1]),\n            )\n        wp.synchronize()\n        t1 = time.perf_counter()\n\n        device_wall_time = t1 - t0\n        total_wall_time += device_wall_time\n        max_wall_time = max(max_wall_time, device_wall_time)\n\n        device_cells = grid_shape[0] * grid_shape[1] * grid_shape[2] * num_steps\n        total_cells_updated += device_cells\n\n per_step_ms=(max_wall_time / num_steps) * 1000,\n        device=f\"multi:{','.join(devices)}\",\n        backend=\"warp\",\n    )\n\n\ndef benchmark_scaling(\n    grid_shape: tuple[int, int, int] = GPUBenchmarkResult]:\n    \"\"\"Compare single-GPU vs multi-GPU scaling.\n\n    Parameters\n    ----------\n    grid_shape : tuple[int, int, int]\n        Grid dimensions (nx, ny, nz).\n    num_steps GPUBenchmarkResult:\n    \"\"\"Run GPU benchmark using multiple GPUs.\n\n    Each GPU runs an independent simulation with the same grid shape.\n    This measures independent GPU throughput, not parallel solving.\n\n    For Phase 1's monolithic (single-chunk) architecture, this represents\n    the scenario where multiple independent simulations are run on multiple\n    GPUs for parameter sweeps or monte carlo studies.\n\n    Parameters\n    ----------\n    grid_shape : tuple[int, int, int]\n        Grid dimensions (nx, ny, nz) for each GPU.\n    num_steps : int, default=100\n        Number of timesteps per GPU.\n    devices : tuple[str, str], default=(\"cuda:0\", \"cuda:1\")\n        Target GPU device strings.\n    dt, dx, dy, dz, warmup_steps\n        Forwarded to ``benchmark_single_gpu``.\n\n    Returns\n    -------\n    GPUBenchmarkResult\n        Aggregated benchmark results from all GPUs (total throughput).\n\n    Raises\n    ------\n    RuntimeError\n        If Warp is not available or GPU execution fails.\n    \"\"\"\n    if not WARP_AVAILABLE:\n        raise RuntimeError(\"Warp is not available; cannot run GPU benchmark\")\n\n    # Run on first GPU to establish baseline\n    # The \"multi-GPU\" case here measures total throughput across multiple GPUs\n    # for independent simulations\n    from autofdtd.kernels.steps import step_maxwell\n\n    total_wall_time = 0.0\n    total_cells_updated = 0\n    max_wall_time = 0.0\n\n    for device in devices:\n        # Set the target device\n        wp.set_device(wp.get_device(device))\n\n        # Allocate arrays on target device\n        arrays = _allocate_gpu_arrays(grid_shape, device=device)\n\n        # Warm up JIT compilation on this device\n        for step in range(warmup_steps):\n            step_maxwell(\n                arrays,\n                dt=dt,\n                dx=dx,\n                dy=dy,\n                dz=dz,\n                step_index=step,\n                device=int(device.split(\":\")[1]),\n            )\n        wp.synchronize()\n\n        # Benchmark run on this device\n        t0 = time.perf_counter()\n        for step in range(num_steps):\n            step_maxwell(\n                arrays,\n                dt=dt,\n                dx=dx,\n                dy=dy,\n                dz=dz,\n                step_index=step,\n                device=int(device.split(\":\")[1]),\n            )\n        wp.synchronize()\n        t1 = time.perf_counter()\n\n        device_wall_time = t1 - t0\n        total_wall_time += device_wall_time\n        max_wall_time = max(max_wall_time, device_wall_time)\n\n        device_cells = grid_shape[0] * grid_shape[1] * grid_shape[2] * num_steps\n        total_cells_updated += device_cells\n\n    total_cells = grid_shape[0] * grid_shape[1] * grid_shape[2]\n    # Use max single-device time for scaling comparison\n    # (total_cells_updated / max_wall_time) gives combined Gcells/s\n    # using the slowest device as the bottleneck\n    gcells_s = (total_cells_updated / max_wall_time) / 1e9\n\n    return GPUBenchmarkResult(\n        grid_shape=grid_shape,\n        total_cells=total_cells,\n        num_steps=num_steps,\n        wall_time_s=max_wall_time,  # Use single-device time for fair comparison\n        cells_updated=total_cells_updated,\n        gcells_per_second=gcells_s,\n        per_step_ms=(max_wall_time / num_steps) * 1000,\n        device=f\"multi:{','.join(devices)}\",\n        backend=\"warp\",\n    )\n\n\ndef benchmark_scaling(\n    grid_shape: tuple[int, int, int] = When we allocate arrays on cuda:1 with `device=device` and then call `step_maxwell` with `device=0`, the global context is still on cuda:1 but the kernel is trying to run on cuda:0.\n\nI need to remove the `wp.set_device` call from `benchmark_multi_gpu` and instead rely on the array's device (which is set by the `device` parameter in `_allocate_gpu_arrays`).
